## Consolidated cross-cutting finding: `.help-block` is set to `display: none !important` app-wide in legacy's CSS, making every Angular "field is required" validation message invisible on every screen that uses this shared pattern — candidate for an entry-55-style standing policy, flagged for owner decision

**Where:** `resources/assets/css/param.common.master.css:8445` — `.help-block{ display: none !important; }`, with no override anywhere raising it back to `display:block` (`.has-error .help-block` rules checked too — only margin/font-size tweaks found, no visibility override). Discovered during batch 11's `reasonMaster`/`reasonTypeMaster` audit.

**What it is, plainly:** every `ng-if="...$error.required"` "X is required" message on every screen using this widely-shared markup pattern is permanently invisible in legacy today, regardless of the `show-errors-check-validity`/`show-errors-reset` broadcasts (those only toggle the separate `has-error` border-highlight class). This is almost certainly true across most or all of the ~90+ already-shipped master screens too, not just batch 11's — a spot-check reference read of an already-shipped screen (`sub-speciality-master.ts`) shows these messages rendered visibly in the rebuild, which is the opposite of legacy's actual (broken) behavior on that exact same markup pattern.

**Not touched in any already-shipped screen — flagged once here rather than re-litigated screen-by-screen**, matching how entry 55 was handled for the shared `<modal>` crash. This needs a single owner decision that then applies consistently: (a) faithfully reproduce the invisibility everywhere (strict parity, but arguably a real, long-standing legacy UX defect nobody intended), or (b) treat it like entry 55/60/61's precedent of disclosed, minor visual improvements and leave validation messages visible going forward (already the de facto behavior across nearly all shipped screens, whether or not each build agent recognized the CSS-suppression cause at the time). No individual screen's rebuild has been altered pending this decision.

**My read:** worth resolving promptly precisely because it's silently already been decided inconsistently, screen by screen, by whichever build agent happened to make its `.help-block` visible or not without knowing about this CSS rule — recommend the same one-time standing-policy treatment entry 55 got, applied retroactively across all shipped screens for consistency, once decided.

**RESOLVED — owner decision 2026-07-22:** adopt option (b) — the rebuild's visible validation messages are a formal, disclosed standing improvement over legacy's broken CSS, same treatment as entry 55. Logged as a standing policy in `docs/ANGULAR_REBUILD_PLAYBOOK.md` (Step 3 section), so this does not need re-litigating screen by screen going forward. No already-shipped screen needs retroactive changes — this confirms the behavior most already had.

---

## 135. SEVERITY 1, FAST-TRACK — `loginController.js:124`'s `getUnitByUserCredentials()` crashes on a real race condition, blocking the Unit dropdown from populating; root cause confirmed via live reproduction as a genuine code bug, NOT a dev-environment data gap

**Where:** `resources/assets/js/controller/login/loginController.js:96-182` (`getUnitByUserCredentials`), triggered by `ng-blur` on the Username/Password fields and `ng-change` on the Organization dropdown (`views/login.html:271,282,291`).

**Root cause, confirmed definitively via live reproduction (not just static reading):** the organization list (`GET /adt/organizationMasterList/localhost`) is fetched asynchronously on page load and only ever assigns `$scope.organizationList`/auto-selects `$scope.organizationId` inside that GET's own `.then()` callback (lines 70-91) — there is no synchronous or `ng-init` initialization anywhere else. `getUnitByUserCredentials()` (line 107) does `$scope.organizationList.find(ar => ar.organizationId == $scope.organizationId)`, then dereferences the result unguarded at **line 124**: `found.organizationLogo`. If a blur/change event fires before that GET has resolved (or resolves to no match), `found` is `undefined` and this throws `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'organizationLogo')` — confirmed live, twice, by directly manipulating the real controller's `$scope` in a running browser session (no credentials entered; synthetic scope state only, matching this project's own established `$controller`-instantiation test methodology): with `organizationList=[]`/`organizationId=''`, calling the real function threw this exact error; restoring the correctly-resolved state (`organizationList=[{organizationId:1,...}]`, `organizationId=1`) and calling the identical function produced zero errors. The thrown exception happens **before** the function ever reaches its real unit-fetch POST (`/adt/getUnitByCredentials`, lines 131-179) — so when it fires, the Unit dropdown never gets a chance to populate for that call.

**Confirmed NOT a data gap:** directly queried the real backend (`curl http://localhost:8080/adt/organizationMasterList/localhost`) — it returns exactly one real, correctly-formed organization (`organizationId: 1, organizationName: "DEMO"`), same as always. The data this screen depends on is completely intact; this is a pure timing/guard bug in the frontend controller, unrelated in kind to the BR-038/039/043/046/100/101/113/115-117 local-dev-DB-drift gap class.

**Why this is hitting real users (including the owner) today, not just an edge case:** browser autofill/password-manager-driven form fills can populate and blur multiple fields in a tight synchronous burst, well inside the organization-list GET's round-trip time, and — critically — nothing else re-triggers `getUnitByUserCredentials()` afterward once the org list does resolve (the auto-selection at line 82 is a plain `$scope` assignment, not a user-driven `ng-change`, so it doesn't re-fire this function). A user whose browser autofills the login form can hit this crash on every attempt with no further recovery event, exactly matching the reported "Unit dropdown won't populate" symptom.

**Immediate, no-code-change workaround available right now:** manually click into the Username field, type, then Tab or click into the Password field (a deliberate pause, even brief, virtually guarantees the sub-100ms organization-list GET has already resolved by the second blur), type the password, then Tab/click away once more before submitting — this gives the function a clean, already-resolved state to run against. Disabling browser-autofill for this one login (or clearing/re-entering the fields manually instead of accepting an autofill suggestion) should unblock login immediately without touching any code.

**Proposed minimal fix (NOT applied — held for owner approval, same fast-track format/precedent as entries 40/43/46/54):**
```js
// line 124, before:
$scope.img = (found.organizationLogo !=null && found.organizationLogo !="") ? found.organizationLogo : "resources/assets/images/logo/loginImgD.png";
// line 124, after:
$scope.img = (found && found.organizationLogo !=null && found.organizationLogo !="") ? found.organizationLogo : "resources/assets/images/logo/loginImgD.png";
```
A single added `found &&` guard, mirroring the guard the same function's author already applied one line earlier (line 108, `if (found && found.differentTypesScreenImageList...)`) and one line later (line 127, `if(found != undefined){...}`) — this dereference is the one spot they missed. This stops the crash and lets execution reach the real unit-fetch POST on whichever blur/change event happens to be the first one where `organizationId` already matches (which, per the analysis above, is very likely to occur naturally on a second field-blur even without any deliberate pause) — it does not change legacy's user-visible behavior in the already-working case, and turns a hard crash into graceful handling in the racing case. **Not a full redesign** of the underlying "no automatic retry once the org list resolves" gap (a debounce/watch-based re-trigger would be a larger, more invasive change) — flagging that as a possible follow-up, not proposing it now, since the one-line guard is sufficient to unblock the crash itself.

**UPDATE — owner approved, implemented and verified, held for review:** applied on a dedicated branch, `fix/logincontroller-getunitbyusercredentials-found-guard` (based on `main`, per this project's legacy-fix precedent — not based on any Angular-rebuild pilot branch). Verified live against the running dev Tomcat instance (temporarily redeployed, then restored to the exact original afterward, per established practice): (1) re-reproduced the original crash-state input (`found` undefined) — confirmed it throws identically on the unfixed code; (2) the same crash-state input against the fixed code now produces **zero console errors**, with `$scope.img` gracefully falling back to the default logo; (3) a resolved-state input (`found` defined) still produces zero errors, `$scope.img` still resolves from `found.organizationLogo` exactly as before, and execution now correctly reaches the real `POST /adt/getUnitByCredentials` call (confirmed via live network capture) — matching normal working behavior with no regression. All three checks used only synthetic `$scope` state and placeholder (non-real) credentials for the resolved-state POST test, per this project's standing no-credentials-entered discipline; no real login was performed. Held on its own branch, not merged to `main`.

**UPDATE — owner approved deploying this fix live:** the single-line fix was deployed to the running dev Tomcat instance (confirmed live via a fresh fetch of the served file). This let the owner's next login attempt reach the real backend for the first time — which then surfaced a second, entirely separate blocker: a genuine local-dev-DB data gap (the environment's only unit was marked inactive), not a further code bug. See `BR-local-dev-db-drift-unitstatus-loginblocked` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`/`docs/BR_INDEX.md` for that fix's full detail — resolved and owner-approved the same session. That investigation also surfaced a separate, non-blocking oddity in this same function worth a future decision: `loginController.js:127-129` sets `data.isDuoUserLogin=true` unconditionally whenever the organization is found, regardless of that organization's own `isDuoAuthenticationRequired` flag — routing every login through the duo-specific backend query variant even when duo/2FA isn't configured. Not fixed, not blocking (the duo variant happens to be the more lenient of the two queries), flagged here for awareness only.

## 136. Step 5 live testing results — batch 11's 15 screens, all verified against the real backend after re-authentication; 2 real rebuild bugs found and fixed, 2 new local dev-DB gaps found (held for approval), 1 dev-tooling proxy gap found and fixed

**Context:** all 15 batch-11 screens were live-tested end-to-end (real save/update/toggle round trips against the actual backend, not just code review) once the owner's login was restored (see entry 135 and `BR-local-dev-db-drift-unitstatus-loginblocked`). Results below, grouped by outcome.

**Confirmed fully working, no new findings:** `holidayCalenderMaster`, `outSideHospitalMaster`, `reasonMaster`, `reasonTypeMaster` (entries 125/126 re-confirmed accurate on live re-verification), `rejectionMaster`, `ICPCMapper`/`icpcCodesConfiguration` (both load clean and call the correct real endpoints; full save round trip untestable in this environment — the ICPC-system source table has zero seed rows, a data gap not a bug), `instructionMaster` (full save round trip confirmed working after the proxy fix below), `consumableBarcodeConfig` (entry 134's duplicate-create bug and the Vendor Name fix both re-confirmed live), `fixedAssetMaster` (entry 134's hardcoded-dropdown and `assetTypeId=0`-on-load bugs both re-confirmed live via network capture; the Clear-button and Manufacturer-field fixes both re-confirmed crash-free; full create round trip untestable — this environment's Asset Category data is empty for the one real Asset Type, a data gap not a bug), `systemMaster`'s severity-1 (entry 128) — re-confirmed live via a controlled mocked-failure test, with visual screenshot evidence of the entire app frozen behind the stuck loader overlay.

**`clinicMaster`:** loads clean, Floor→Wing cascading dropdown logic works correctly, but `adt.m_wing_master` has zero rows in this dev DB (confirmed via direct query) — no way to construct a valid Floor+Wing combination to test the save flow, and no existing row to test edit/toggle. Coverage gap, not a bug; no wing data fabricated without approval.

**`assetTypeMaster`:** entry 133's severity-1 fix (real backend call instead of the dead `constants` reference) works — the screen no longer crashes on load. But a real save attempt surfaced two further, previously-undiscovered issues (both below).

### New finding — a genuine rebuild bug, not a legacy parity target, found and fixed (not held for approval)

`asset-type-master.ts`'s `create()` sent `unitId: 0, organizationId: 0, userId: 0` as literal hardcoded zeros. This was based on a doc-comment claim that "this screen needs no cookie values" — the same class of misread as entry 120's retracted `areaMaster` finding. Re-reading `AssetTypeService.create` (java) directly showed the real mechanism: it null-coalesces each field to the *backend's own* `sessionService.get*()` only when the request **omits** the field (`!= null`). Legacy's own `CreateAssetType()` sends none of these three fields at all (confirmed via direct source read) — letting the backend's session-derived fallback take over. Since the rebuild sent literal `0`s (not `null`/omitted), the backend used those zeros verbatim instead of falling back to the real session values. Fixed to send the real, session-resolved values (`this.session.organizationId` etc.), matching the established, working convention every other screen in this batch already uses. Verified live: the save payload now correctly sends `unitId:1, organizationId:1, userId:1`. Not held for owner approval — matching legacy's actual (correct) behavior is the task itself, same reasoning as entry 120's fix.

### New finding — a local dev-DB gap, held for owner approval, not applied

Even with the fix above, a real save attempt on `assetTypeMaster` still 500s. Root cause confirmed via `catalina.out` + direct DB query: `m_asset_type_id_seq`'s sequence is out of sync with the table's real data — `last_value` is `1`, but the table already has 2 real rows (`id` 1 and 2, seeded 2018-05-29), so the next `nextval()` call collides on the existing `id=1` primary key. Same gap class as entry 116 (`m_action_master`/`action_master_seq`, batch 10). Proposed fix, not applied: `SELECT setval('m_asset_type_id_seq', (SELECT MAX(id) FROM m_asset_type))`.

### New finding — a local dev-DB gap, held for owner approval, not applied

`systemObservationMaster`'s real save attempt 500s. Root cause confirmed via `catalina.out`: `column "speciality_ids" of relation "m_system_observation_master" does not exist` — the Java entity (`SystemObervationMaster.java:65-66`, `@Column(name="speciality_ids") private String specialityIds;`) expects a column that was never created on `emr.m_system_observation_master` in this local dev DB (confirmed via `\d`). Same gap class as every other `BR-local-dev-db-drift-*` entry this session. Proposed fix, not applied: `ALTER TABLE emr.m_system_observation_master ADD COLUMN speciality_ids character varying(255)`.

### Dev-tooling gap found and fixed directly (not an application-behavior change, same precedent as prior batches' proxy-config fixes)

`instructionMaster` threw a real, reproducible `HttpErrorResponse` console error on every load. Root cause: `new/proxy.conf.json`/`proxy.conf.example.json` never had a `/LIS/**` rule, so `GET /LIS/global/listLanguageMaster/1/1` fell through to the Angular dev server's own SPA-fallback `index.html` (HTTP 200, `content-type: text/html`) instead of reaching the real backend — Angular's `HttpClient` then failed to parse HTML as JSON. Added `/LIS/**` to both proxy config files (same fix shape as the `/mrdrack/**`/`/opd/**` gaps in batches 7/9), restarted `ng serve`, re-verified: zero console errors, `listLanguageMaster` now correctly reaches the real backend.

**UPDATE — both DB gaps approved and resolved:** owner approved both fixes ("Yes, approve both DB fixes — go ahead and apply them"). `SELECT setval('m_asset_type_id_seq', (SELECT MAX(id) FROM m_asset_type))` and `ALTER TABLE emr.m_system_observation_master ADD COLUMN speciality_ids character varying(255)` both applied. Verified live: `assetTypeMaster` save now returns `201`/`{"id":3,...}`; `systemObservationMaster` save now returns `status:success`. Full detail in `BR-local-dev-db-drift-assettype-seq`/`BR-local-dev-db-drift-systemobservation-specialityids` (`docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`/`docs/BR_INDEX.md`).

## 137. 12 more confirmed instances of the "administrative-sounding module, actually clinical/billing content" mistagging pattern, found during batch 12 candidate pre-screening — same class as `doorToNeedle`/entry 66, `serviceMaster`/entry 103, and the 6-screen entry 118

**Where:** caught during batch 12's candidate research pass (an agent-assisted sweep of the confirmed-clean pool, independently spot-checked by direct source read on a sample before trusting the full list, per this project's standing "verify before claiming parity" discipline).

**All 12 confirmed via source, not just Phase 0's automated keyword sweep:**
1. **`orderSetMaster`** — tagged Pharmacy/no clinical. Controller (`controller/orderset/orderSetMasterController.js`) has real `doctorId`/`diagnosisId` fields and a full `medication`/`medications` object (dose/route/frequency/generic) — a clinical order-set builder, confirmed via direct read (`grep` hits at lines 38, 41, 53, 76, 79).
2. **`growthChartMaster`** — controller physically lives in `controller/clinical/`.
3. **`editReportedNabh`** — full of `patientDetails`/`encounterId`/`admissionId`/`diagnosisId`.
4. **`nabhProcess`** — calls `/api/global/coversheet/templates` and hardcodes the UI label "Antibiotic Addition" — clinical.
5. **`videoConferenceNew`** — genuinely schedules doctor↔patient telemedicine video-consult invites (`$scope.patientsMails`, `$scope.doctorMails`) — clinical.
6. **`MRDfileManagement`** — controller in `controller/otims/global/`, full of `patientId`/`encounterId`/`admissionId`.
7. **`autoServicePostingConfiguration`** — real billing endpoints (`/billing/service/autoRendered/`, `/config/billing/master`) — billing-tier.
8. **`ambulanceRouting`** — controller lives in `controller/lis/global/`, a suspicious folder for an "ambulance" screen — flagged for individual investigation, not yet fully characterized.
9-12. **`genericScheduleMaster`, `formulationTypeROAMaster`, `routeAdministration`, `pharmacyproductCategoryMaster`** — all four controllers physically live in `controller/master/pharmacy/`; `genericScheduleMaster`'s content independently confirmed as real medication-scheduling (generic drug name → legal drug schedule, "Is Emergency Medicine" flag) — pharmacy-tier, same class as entry 118's `itemMasterGlobal`.

**Also flagged during the same pass, structurally complex/coupled rather than mistagged (excluded from batch selection for a different reason, noted for the record, not re-queued as Phase 5/6 candidates the way the above are):**
- `commonMaster` (`/commonMaster/:url`) — a dynamic dispatcher resolving to entirely different controllers depending on a URL param; not one screen.
- `call` (`QMSCallMasterController`) — the inventory doc's field/API count badly undercounts it; the real file is an 871-line, Pubnub-integrated real-time token-calling console (same risk shape as `display`, already excluded from earlier batches for this reason).
- `assetPMSScheduler` — otherwise clean, but its view has a live (not commented-out) "Insurance Details" tab with a hardcoded sample row — a literal "insurance" keyword hit in rendered UI, excluded per the strict content-check policy even though the underlying data-fetch is dead and the insurance concept is asset-insurance, not patient-insurance. Flagged as a borderline case an owner might reasonably choose to include later.
- `dischargeFieldMapper` — genuinely just discharge-summary field-metadata mapping (no real patient content), excluded out of family-resemblance caution since it directly feeds the same pipeline as the already-excluded discharge-summary cluster, not a hard content hit itself.
- `incidentReportingScreen` — title is literally "NABH Incident Reporting," same family as the excluded nabh cluster.
- `fixedAssetAddAcessory`, `assetIssueDirectIssue`, `detailsAssetIssueList` — all three depend on a shared `commonDetailService.getDataId()/setDataId()` singleton to hand off a record ID from a different, unbuilt/excluded screen; not independently testable.
- `assetIssueMaster`, `DetailsAssetPendingIssue` — both have real, live `patientId` fields / a `patientSearchByMultipleCriteria()` function — content-based patient hits despite "no/no" Phase 0 tags. **These two should be added to the mistagged list above (13-14) — genuine clinical-content hits, not just structural complexity.**
- `transferRequestDemo` — literally bracketed `/** Demo Added By Vivek **/ ... /** Demo End **/` in `app.js`; not a real feature.
- The Asset-Management "auction"/"gate-pass" cluster (`auctionRequistion`, `nonReturnableGatePass`, `itemsForAuction`, etc.) — multi-role approval workflows with the same `commonDetailService` coupling, out of scope for a low-risk batch, not individually verified further.

**Action taken:** the 14 confirmed-mistagged screens (12 originally listed + `assetIssueMaster`/`DetailsAssetPendingIssue` reclassified from "structural" to "content hit") are excluded from the low-risk buildable pool, same treatment as `doorToNeedle`/`serviceMaster`/entry 118 — re-queued as Phase 5/6 candidates, not for casual low-risk batch selection. None built, none selected into batch 12.

**My read:** this is now the 3rd occurrence of a dedicated sweep turning up a double-digit batch of mistagged screens (doorToNeedle → serviceMaster+6 → this 14), all caught only by content-based verification during actual batch pre-screening, never by the automated Phase 0 sweep itself. The recurring theme this time: controller **folder location** (`controller/clinical/`, `controller/pharmacy/`, `controller/lis/`) is turning out to be a more reliable red flag than the module tag or screen name in several of these — worth considering as an additional automated heuristic for a future sweep, alongside the existing keyword/endpoint-path check.

## 138. Phase 4 batch 12 — Step 0/1 audit findings across all 7 screens (`qualityParameterMaster`, `roomModality`, `userAuthentication`, `QMS`, `QMSMasterCall`, `Qmslogo`, `PMRequest`) — logged, not fixed except where noted

**Context:** consolidated findings from each screen's full Step 0 (confirm-real) + Step 1 (audit) pass, per `docs/ANGULAR_REBUILD_PLAYBOOK.md`'s checklist, done in parallel with real authenticated-session backend-liveness verification. Two live security findings surfaced during this pass were fast-tracked as their own documents rather than logged here: `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_UserAuthenticationPlaintextPasswordDisclosure.md` and `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_QmslogoAnonymousWriteAccess.md`. Two local-dev-DB gaps found during this pass (`qualityParameterMaster`'s entirely-missing `t_quality_parameter_master` table, `QMS`'s missing `qms_prn_print_format` column) were owner-approved and resolved — see `BR-local-dev-db-drift-qualityparametermaster-missingtable` and `BR-local-dev-db-drift-qms-prnprintformat` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`. Everything below is either informational (log-only) or a rebuild-time decision within normal Step 3 discretion (no behavioral/business-logic deviation).

**`qualityParameterMaster`:**
- `controllerAs:'vm'` is declared in the state config but never actually used — the view is 100% `$scope.*` bindings (entry-11 pattern recurrence). Build as classic `$scope`-pattern.
- HTML5 `required` on 2 `<select>` elements is inert (no `<form>` wrapper) — the only real enforcement is the manual JS block in `saveQualityParam()`. Port that JS block faithfully; the HTML5 attributes don't need separate treatment.
- Real load-order bug: `editQualityParam()` can fire before its two dropdown-lookup calls resolve, silently leaving `selectedUOMId`/`selectModuleId` as `undefined` with no visible error. Legacy bug — rebuild should guard defensively (disable/hide edit until lookups resolve) rather than port the race literally.
- A sibling `@NamedNativeQuery` (`GET_QUALITY_PARAMETER_MASTER_BY_ID`) has a SQL syntax bug (missing `WHERE` keyword) — dead code, never called by this screen's frontend, not a blocker.
- Widespread cosmetic copy-paste artifact (wrong page title / wrong sidebar-highlight target in a trailing `<script>` block) shared by 12 other `organizationMaster/` screens — cosmetic only, not a rebuild concern (Angular Router handles active-nav state natively).

**`roomModality`:**
- New instance of the entry-55 shared-`<modal>`-directive `cancel()` gap — the directive's hardcoded close button calls `cancel()`, which this controller never defines. Not a rebuild blocker per the existing standing policy; adding to that catalog for completeness (screen wasn't part of the original 49-screen sweep).
- Copy-pasted validation messages reference a nonexistent field (`modalityForm.ward` — the controller's own JSDoc still says `wardMasterController`, confirming the copy-paste origin) on all 3 required-field messages. Currently masked by the global `.help-block` CSS bug; since the rebuild renders validation messages visibly by default (owner-approved standing policy), **binding these messages to the screen's real field names (`roomModalityId`/`subSpecialityId`/`modalityId`) during Step 3 is treated as implementing that existing standing policy correctly, not a new behavioral deviation requiring separate sign-off** — a message wired to a field that doesn't exist on this form can never be part of any meaningful "parity," visible or not.
- A real, live, reproducible `TypeError` in legacy today: `cookieObjectAppConfig` is dereferenced with no null-check in `init()`, distinct from (but same severity class as) entry 9. Legacy bug, not a rebuild blocker — rebuild uses a defensive check per standing convention.
- Two sibling screens confirmed as non-functional mockups during this audit, found incidentally: `roomModulityMapper` (wired to shared `MasterController`, zero live bindings) and `doctorPayOuts` (wired to `roomModalityController` itself — a mismatched-controller reuse — with a fully static view). Neither is in this batch; logging as decision points for whoever eventually picks them up, same treatment as `addItemPOCreation`/`SurgicalSiteInfection`/`OTBillingCompletion`.
- The screen's search `<input>` has no `ng-model`/`ng-change` at all — decorative/dead in legacy. Faithfully reproduced as non-functional unless the owner wants it wired up as a disclosed improvement (not assumed).

**`userAuthentication`:**
- The status/active-toggle column and Role column are both fully commented out in the live view — there is no live toggle or role-assignment UI on this screen today. The rebuild must not add a status toggle here (would over-deliver relative to legacy parity), unlike most master screens in this project.
- Confirmed sibling divergence: `userRoleManagement` has a live, working status toggle on structurally identical markup — informational only, this screen's own toggle stays dead per the point above.
- `$scope.sendMail`/`/api/global/sendPasswordToUser` is orphaned/unreachable from this screen's own view (only a same-named function in an unrelated screen calls the same backend endpoint) — already covered under the password-disclosure DECISION_NEEDED doc's endpoint family, no separate action.

**`QMS` / `QMSMasterCall`:**
- Both controllers share an identical, real validation bug in `confirmQMS()`: `if($scope.common.qmsKioskMasterId!="" || $scope.common.qmsKioskMasterId!=undefined)` is a tautology (always true for any value) — the kiosk-selection guard never actually blocks an empty selection. Faithfully reproduce; do not silently correct the operator without owner sign-off.
- Confirmed, deliberate functional divergence to preserve: `QMS` sets `$rootScope.loginpage = false` (renders full-screen, no nav chrome — kiosk mode); `QMSMasterCall` sets it `true` (normal admin chrome). The rebuild must not collapse these into one uniform `Shell`-wrapped treatment.
- `QMS-master-call.html`'s trailing sidebar-highlight script targets a link matching `"QMS"`, which never matches `QMSMasterCall`'s own link — stale copy-paste, harmless (Angular Router's own active-link handling sidesteps this entirely in the rebuild).

**`Qmslogo`:**
- Not a standard text-field master — 6 of 8 fields are real file-upload inputs, and even the 2 text fields (Application Name, Copy Write Name) submit via `multipart/form-data` to the same endpoint rather than a normal JSON `GenericService` call. Step 3 needs real multipart handling, `FileReader`-based image preview, and matching client-side format/size validation — not the standard scaffold's JSON save pattern.
- Confirmed validation-duplication bug: the shared `validfile` directive is registered twice (5MB and 25MB variants) on the same file inputs; the 5MB handler's `elem.val('')` clears the native `FileList` before the 25MB handler's check runs, so the 25MB variant is dead in practice — the real effective limit is 5MB. Preserve both layers' presence in the rebuild, but the enforced behavior to port is the 5MB limit.
- (The anonymous-write backend gap is tracked separately in `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_QmslogoAnonymousWriteAccess.md` — not repeated here.)

**`PMRequest`:**
- Confirmed **no** `commonDetailService`-coupling to an unbuilt companion screen (the pattern that excluded `assetReturn` and the entry-137 cluster) — every "details" interaction is a same-page modal populated by this screen's own controller. Independently buildable, stays in the batch.
- **Live, reproducible 500** on `GET /api/unit/vendors/{unitId}/{organizationId}` (the "PMS Details" modal's Vendor tab) — isolated to `VendorController`'s own HQL query via a live sibling-endpoint comparison, unrelated to auth or the finding below. A real, currently-broken legacy backend call, independent of this rebuild — flagging for the owner's awareness; not something a frontend rebuild can fix.
- **Real, shared legacy bug, faithfully reproduce:** `pmRequestController.js`'s `VENDOR_MASTER_API` call sends `(unitId, organizationId)` as path segments, but `VendorController.java` maps them positionally as `(orgId, unitId)` — a silent argument swap. Confirmed byte-identical in 2 sibling controllers too (`preventiveMaintenanceController.js`, `pmScheduleController.js`) — a pre-existing cluster-wide defect, masked in this dev DB only because `organizationId == unitId == 1` here. Port as-is; do not silently correct.
- `getAssetDetailsByCriteria`'s client-side result filter uses `||` across 5 independent search criteria (month/year/store/item/status) instead of what looks like an intended `&&` — near-any row survives the filter. Pre-existing legacy behavior, worth a second look by the owner, faithfully reproduced pending any decision.
- No module-level route gate exists in legacy for this screen (module 224 is nav-visibility-only, confirmed via `checkAccess()`'s UI-only effect) — recommend wiring `data.moduleId: 224` in the rebuild's route guard anyway, consistent with the already-established `billingGroupMaster` precedent (a disclosed improvement, not a parity violation, no separate sign-off needed per Step 3's existing convention).

**Action taken:** all of the above are either logged for the owner's general awareness (legacy bugs unrelated to this rebuild's own correctness, or decision points for other, not-yet-built screens) or fall within Step 3's normal rebuild discretion (dead/inert UI ported as dead/inert, shared-directive gaps covered by the existing entry-55 policy, validation-message field-binding corrected as part of the already-approved visibility standing policy). None require a new owner decision beyond the two DECISION_NEEDED docs and two DB fixes already handled separately this session.

## 139. Phase 4 batch 12 — Step 4/5 (golden-trace re-audit + live interaction testing) results across all 7 screens

**Context:** live testing against the real backend with a real authenticated session, per `docs/ANGULAR_REBUILD_PLAYBOOK.md` Steps 4/5. One credential-disclosure finding surfaced incidentally during this pass was fast-tracked separately: `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_UnitApplicationConfigCredentialDisclosure.md`. Two new local-environment gaps were found and are proposed for owner approval below (not yet applied).

**`qualityParameterMaster` — PASS, all 5 interaction checks clean** (create, 8-field sequential validation, status toggle, edit flow, edit-guard). One new environment finding: `lab.m_unit_master` has zero seed rows for `org_id=1`, leaving the UOM dropdown genuinely empty — blocks a fully real-data create/edit test for that one field, same class as the existing `insuranceMaster`/`MISReports`/`inputOutputCharts` seed-gap caveats. Separately noted: the backend accepts a non-existent `uomId` on save with no referential-integrity check — low severity in this dev environment, flagged for awareness given the billing-accuracy priority in `CLAUDE.md`, not acted on.

**`roomModality` — 2 real findings, 1 new rebuild-introduced bug:**
- Golden-trace mismatch: the rebuild's `radiologySpecialityId === ''` guard (added to avoid a crash on the null-cookie case) also suppresses `getSubSpecialityBySpecialityId`/`getModalityBySubSpecialityId` entirely when the org's `radiologySpecialityId` config is legitimately `null` (not just undefined) — legacy calls these endpoints unconditionally. In this dev DB both approaches return the same empty result, but the guard is broader than its "don't crash" intent and fully blocks the cascading Room→Sub-Speciality→Modality dropdown chain whenever this org config is null. Medium severity — a real, disclosed behavioral divergence from legacy, not yet resolved.
- `getRoomModalityList` returns 0 rows while `updateRoomModalityMasterCount` reports 1 — a backend/dev-DB data-integrity mismatch, blocking full create-flow live verification (could not be resolved with real data).
- **New bug found in the rebuild itself:** on a *failed* status-toggle attempt (tested via a synthetic invalid-id row, since no real rows were available), the native checkbox visually flips before the failure response arrives and is never reasserted to the correct state — narrower than entry 12's original bug (that was about a *successful* toggle not updating the UI; this is the mirror case, a *failed* toggle incorrectly updating the UI). Low-medium severity, only manifests on the error path. Not fixed — held for owner awareness, same treatment as other UI-polish findings this batch.
- **Operational note:** a testing agent deleted the shared `cookieObjectAppConfig` browser cookie while verifying the defensive-guard fix (confirming no crash, which passed). It was restored from the same live config endpoint's non-sensitive fields before the remaining screens were tested; no lasting effect confirmed.

**`userAuthentication` — PASS, all 6 checks clean** (after a stale `localStorage.permissions` cache — missing module 676 — was refreshed from the real `/privilege/role` backend endpoint; this was a client-side cache-staleness issue, not a DB or code gap, and required no owner approval to resolve). Zero password exposure confirmed both visually and structurally. Pagination check is data-limited (only 1 seed row exists) rather than failed.

**`QMS`/`QMSMasterCall` — PASS on both, after the module-794 fix.** `QMS`: clean golden-trace match, `getQMSPrnFormat`'s earlier DB fix confirmed live, tautology-guard bug and unguarded-`list[0]`-access crash both confirmed faithfully reproduced, matching legacy exactly. `QMSMasterCall`: was blocked (module **794** genuinely absent from `role_id=1`'s real privilege data — not a cache-staleness issue, confirmed directly against the DB-stored JSON blob). **RESOLVED, owner-approved:** applied the additive privilege grant below, refreshed the session, re-verified live — screen now loads normally (no access-denied redirect), `getActiveQMSMasterList` fires and matches the golden trace, and the same tautology-guard/unguarded-`list[0]`-access bugs are confirmed faithfully present here too (a synthetic empty-selection change event reproduced both, matching `QMS`'s own behavior exactly). The new-tab launch (`window.open('/QMS','_blank')`) and full real-kiosk flow remain unverified — this dev environment has zero seeded QMS kiosk rows, a separate, pre-existing data gap, not something fabricated to force a pass.

**`Qmslogo` — PASS on load/golden-trace and 7 of 8 interaction checks** (independent per-field saves, shared single-`fileError` bug faithfully reproduced, text-field saves both clean). **Real backend bug found, blocks all 6 image-field save round trips:** `DifferentTypesScreenImageDaoImpl.saveScreenImage` (`legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/dao/DifferentTypesScreenImageDaoImpl.java:45-50`) does `Integer bucketType = ...; if (... && bucketType == 1)` with no null-guard — a live NPE on auto-unboxing when `bucket_flag` is `NULL` (confirmed: `t_unit_application_billing_configuration`'s row for org=1/unit=1 has `bucket_flag` genuinely `NULL` in this DB). Every other call site of this exact pattern in the codebase (`DocUploadUtility.java`, ~25 instances) has `if (bucketType == null) bucketType = 1;` — this DAO is the sole exception, confirmed via full-file grep. A real, isolated legacy Java bug, not a rebuild defect or a dev-seed-data-only issue (a production unit with an unset `bucket_flag` would hit the identical crash). Proposed fix below, held for approval.

**`PMRequest` — PASS on load, golden-trace, and the store-session defensive guard** (confirmed live: legacy's real, unconditional `cookieObjectStore.storeId` deref crashes in a golden-trace capture exactly as predicted; the rebuild's defensive guard lets all 4 initial-load calls complete with zero console errors). **Full interaction testing (search/filter, all 4 modals) blocked**: this dev environment has zero store rows for unitId=1/organizationId=1, so the mandatory store-selection popup can never be dismissed. Same class as the playbook's existing caveat #2 (`implantMaster`/`organizationMaster` real-data gaps) — not a rebuild defect, no data fabricated to force a pass.

---

### Two new fixes proposed for owner approval (neither applied yet)

**1. Grant module 794 to role_id=1's privilege data**, unblocking `QMSMasterCall` live testing:
```sql
UPDATE privilege.privilege
SET modules = (modules::jsonb || '[{"id":794,"name":"QMS Token Display","parentId":791,"moduleType":1}]'::jsonb)::text
WHERE role_id = 1;
```
Module 794 ("QMS Token Display") is already confirmed (Step 0/1 audit) to be the real, live moduleId gating `QMSMasterCall`'s legacy resolve block and is already used correctly by the already-shipped `displayCall` screen — this just adds the missing grant to this dev environment's sole seed role, matching its siblings 795/796/797 (QMS Kiosk/Department/Counter Master, same `parentId: 791` group) which are already present. Additive, reversible (the appended array element can be removed), local-dev-DB only.

**2. Add the missing `bucketType == null` guard to `DifferentTypesScreenImageDaoImpl.saveScreenImage`**, matching the established convention used ~25 times elsewhere in the codebase:
```java
// legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/dao/DifferentTypesScreenImageDaoImpl.java:45-46, before:
Integer bucketType = (Integer) sessionFactory.getCurrentSession()
        .createSQLQuery("select bucket_flag from t_unit_application_billing_configuration where unit_id = :unitId and organization_id = :organizationId")
        ... // (existing query setup)
// after, insert immediately following the query execution:
if (bucketType == null) {
    bucketType = 1;
}
```
This is application code (`legacy/`), not local-dev-DB-only — per `CLAUDE.md`, requires its own dedicated branch (Maker-Checker), separate from the Angular rebuild branch, same treatment as the `loginController.js` race-condition fix (entry 135) and the `organizationController`/`assetCategoryMaster`/`implantMaster` DI-crash fixes.

**Action taken:** module-794 grant owner-approved, applied, live-verified (see `BR-local-dev-db-drift-role1-module794` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`). The `bucketType == null` guard fix was owner-approved separately and implemented on its own dedicated branch, `fix/qmslogo-bucketflag-npe` (based on `main`) — commit `fab84ac`. **Verified live**: deployed to the running dev Tomcat instance, confirmed the NPE no longer occurs (a test save request now proceeds past the `bucketType` check entirely and fails later, at a different, unrelated point — `relation "public.t_service_identity" does not exist`, a separate, previously-undiscovered dev-environment gap in the same method, not chased further without approval). Jars restored to their original state afterward; the fix is held on its own branch, not merged, not deployed persistently, per Maker-Checker discipline.

Clinical-tier.

Recommend a case-insensitive, cross-referenced (not single-source) exclusion check become the default going forward, not an ad hoc correction per batch.

Same bug shape as `bedBook` (entry 53), far larger in scope. **Not characterized further this pass — flagged for a dedicated sweep, same treatment as entry 80's GlobalMaster investigation, before any future batch treats this cluster as available inventory.** None selected into batch 13.

**Worth the owner's own attention, not just a mistagging/exclusion note — found on `nurseDashboard` (already excluded from batch 13 selection, not proposed):** `nurseDashboardMasterController.js`'s `openTriageNurse()`/`openTriageReceptionist()` (live-wired via `ng-click` in `nurse-dashboard.html:198,205`, confirmed via direct read) POST to a real, hardcoded external Supabase Edge Function URL (`https://gpxucwzdeijkcnygwakq.supabase.co/functions/v1/generate-triage-session` / `generate-receptionist-session`) with a hardcoded `userId` GUID (`5c8e0c35-436d-4bd9-bd08-ed9944d2fb3c`), a hardcoded `environment: "qa"` string, and (for the receptionist variant) a hardcoded `workspace: "axoncare"` — then embeds the response's `webhookUrl` directly as a trusted iframe (`$sce.trustAsResourceUrl`) showing a "Virtual Nurse"/"Virtual Secretary" AI session. No API key/auth header visible in the call itself. This is live, clickable code today, not dead/commented-out. Flagged here for the record; not elevated to a DECISION_NEEDED document without the owner's read first, since unlike the session's 3 credential-disclosure findings, this is a different kind of concern (third-party data flow / hardcoded tenant identity / a dev-environment flag baked into shipped code) that may warrant a different kind of investigation than "role check missing."

## 141. Phase 4 batch 13 — Step 4/5 (golden-trace re-audit + live interaction testing) results across all 6 screens; 2 real rebuild bugs found and fixed

**Context:** live testing against the real backend with a real authenticated session, per `docs/ANGULAR_REBUILD_PLAYBOOK.md` Steps 4/5. Both bugs found below were narrowly-scoped rebuild-implementation bugs with unambiguous correct behavior — fixed directly, not held for approval, matching this project's established precedent (e.g. entry 136's 2 batch-11 fixes, the `areaMaster` cityId fix, the `PMRequest` crash guard).

**`resetPwdconfirmPwd` — PASS**, all validation layers verified against the golden trace and live (empty-field cascade, complexity check, differs-from-existing, confirm-match). Also carries this session's separately-reported `resetPassword` identity-binding security fix (`docs/DECISION_NEEDED_ResetPasswordMissingIdentityBinding.md`) — live-verified independently of this batch's Step 4/5 pass: own-userId-wrong-password correctly returns "Existing password is incorrect."; different-userId correctly returns "You are not authorized to change this user's password."

**`dashboardInfo`/`doctorDashboardInfo`/`nurseDashboardInfo` — Step 4 clean on all 3** (golden-trace calls match 1:1). **1 real bug found and fixed:** the ported legacy back-link anchors (bare `<a href="#/dashboard">` etc.) don't perform real Angular navigation and were reproducibly (3/3 real physical clicks) mis-navigating to the app's default route (`billingGroupMaster`) instead of the intended parent dashboard. Fixed: `doctorDashboardInfo` now does real `Router.navigate(['/doctorDashboard'])` (target already built, same batch); `dashboardInfo`/`nurseDashboardInfo` now show a `NotificationService.warning()` disclosing their target (`dashboard`/`nurseDashboard`) isn't built yet, since neither is. Commit `554c92f` on `rebuild/phase4-batch13`. Verified live post-fix: `doctorDashboardInfo`'s link cleanly reaches `/doctorDashboard`; `dashboardInfo`'s link shows the warning toast instead of mis-navigating (`nurseDashboardInfo` shares identical code, covered by the same verification).

**`home`/`doctorDashboard` — Step 4 clean on both** (all load-time API calls match the golden trace 1:1, live-confirmed via network-tab inspection, zero missing/extra/mismatched calls). **1 real bug found and fixed, more severe — could fully lock a user out of the app:** an account with 0 configured stores got a permanently stuck, empty, undismissable store-selection modal on both screens (no Cancel/X, backdrop eats all clicks, Ok only shows a validation warning) — blocking every other interactive element on the page. Root cause, confirmed against legacy source directly: the shared `StoreSelectionService`'s "caller shows the modal synchronously" call-site contract is legacy-faithful only for the ~25 controllers that themselves call `.modal('show')` synchronously right after `storePopup()` (already-merged screens `viewAssetScheduler`/`pmRequest`/5 others use this pattern correctly — this is the already-owner-reviewed entry 37 behavior and is unchanged by this fix). `home`/`doctorDashboard`'s own legacy controllers (`nurseDashboardMasterController.js`, `doctorDashboardController.js`) have no such synchronous call — they rely solely on `storePopup()`'s internal `stores.length > 1` gate, which legacy never shows for 0 or 1 stores. Both rebuild files incorrectly copied the "show synchronously" pattern anyway. Fixed: moved the `show()` call inside the shared service's own `stores.length > 1` branch (harmless/idempotent no-op for the 7 already-merged callers, which still call `show()` themselves too) and removed the incorrect synchronous `show()` call from `home.ts`/`doctor-dashboard.ts` only. Commit `fa6f2c4` on `rebuild/phase4-batch13`. Verified live post-fix: both screens load cleanly with 0 stores configured, no stuck modal, all load-time calls still 200/match.
- Also live-verified post-fix: `doctorDashboard`'s quick-link tiles navigate correctly (tested "Nursing" → `doctorDashboardInfo`, and the back-link roundtrip back to `/doctorDashboard`); the Finance/Administration/Operations/Task/Notification panel "expand" icons correctly do nothing on click, matching legacy's no-`ng-click` markup (non-interactive, decorative-only).
- **Coverage gap, not a bug:** the nursing-station-selection and counter-selection modals (on both `home` and `doctorDashboard`) could not be live-exercised — this dev environment's test account doesn't have data that triggers either flow (`nursingStationList` resolves without prompting a modal; counter isn't applicable for this user). Same class as this project's existing seed-data-gap caveats (e.g. entry 139's `PMRequest`/`qualityParameterMaster` gaps). `CounterSelectionService`'s gating logic was confirmed correct by code inspection (`visible.set(true)` only when `list.length > 1`, matching legacy's `getCounterDetails()`) but not visually confirmed live.

**Action taken:** both bugs fixed, build-verified, and live-verified on `rebuild/phase4-batch13` (commits `554c92f`, `fa6f2c4`). All 6 screens through Steps 0-7, held on `rebuild/phase4-batch13` for owner review — not merged/pushed. **Update: owner-approved and merged into `main`** (`61edd92` rebuild, `606b9a5`/`1509f52` the 2 security fix merges), pushed to `origin/main`.

## 142. Batch 14 candidate research — 2 new mistagging instances, 1 suspected mistag, 2 new rebuild-relevant bugs found on candidate screens, and a strategic finding: the own-controller low-risk pool is nearly exhausted

**Context:** wider-search candidate research for batch 14, same discipline as every prior batch (real-bindings pre-screening, source-level verification, not keyword-only). Started from the 129 not-yet-built, own-controller, Phase-0-tagged-low-risk screens; removed everything already excluded by entries 66/77/78/80/85/102/103/118/119/137/140/141, leaving 62 genuinely unassessed candidates; read essentially all 62 (controller + view, not just names).

**New confirmed mistagging instances, same class as `doorToNeedle`/entry 66, `serviceMaster`/entry 103, entries 118/137/140:**
- **`patientCategory`** (tagged Admin - Global Master) — controller lives at `controller/master/global/billing/patientCategoryController.js`, feeds patient-category-based tariff/billing logic, the same concept the already-excluded `globalServiceMaster` keys billing off. Billing-tier.
- **`transactionLog`** (tagged Admin - Global Master) — controller at `controller/billing/transaction/billingTransactionLogController.js`, calls `/api/billing/transaction/logs` via `BillingGenericService`. Unambiguously billing-tier.

**Suspected but not confirmed mistag, flagged for a dedicated read before any future batch considers it:** `appointmentList` (+ `.home`/`.services`/`.missedcall` sub-states) — controller folder (`controller/opd/transection/`) matches the same folder family as the already-confirmed-clinical self-registration/kiosk cluster (entry 140), tagged "Common/Shared" but very likely patient-appointment-facing. Not independently verified further this pass.

**2 new bugs found on candidate screens (not fixed, not currently in any batch — logged for whichever future batch picks these screens up):**
- **`wardStoreMapper`** (proposed for batch 14): `$scope.wardStoremappedList()` is called in the save/update/status-toggle success handlers (`wardStoreMapperController.js:221,261,351`) but is never defined — its only definition lives in a commented-out block (`:169-189`). Live `ReferenceError` on every successful save/update/status-toggle today, right after the success toast. To be faithfully reproduced if this screen is built, not silently fixed.
- **`detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceScheduleWorklist`** (considered, flagged borderline, not in the final 10 below): `cookieObjectAppConfig.assetMaintenanceSpecialityId` is dereferenced one line *before* the `cookieObject == undefined` login-redirect guard runs — a live, reproducible crash-on-missing-cookie, same bug family as the `roomModality`/entry-9 missing-null-check class.

**Also noted, not a mistag — a complexity/domain-sensitivity flag:** `tallyInterfacing` (Admin - Unit Master tag) is real and content-clean (no PHI) but is a large (1,096-line), complex external-system integration doing live XML round-trips to a Tally ERP host, touching IP/OP/doctor-share accounting ledgers. Phase 0's keyword sweep doesn't flag it, but it reads as a Phase 5/6-tier candidate on complexity/domain-sensitivity grounds, not a quick-win screen. Not selected.

**Large, previously-uncharacterized dead/static-mockup `MasterController` cluster (entry 140):** re-confirmed still unaddressed (~85 shared-controller screens remain behind it, ~70 in the exact cluster entry 140 described). Not drawn from this pass either, per that entry's own standing caution.

**Strategic finding — the single most actionable result of this research pass:** of the 62 individually-read candidates, only 7 came back clean at low complexity, 2 more clean-but-oversized/borderline, and 1 clean-but-`$stateParams`-coupled/oversized (this batch's proposed 10) — roughly 52 more were disqualified this session (6 new mistags/complexity-outliers above, the 13-screen auction cluster already out-of-scope per entry 137, ~4 bed-cluster screens already on `ANGULAR_PARKED_SCREENS.md`, ~9 parked-list/clinical-suspect screens, ~4 dead/empty routes, ~13 other individually-verified rejects). **No clean second batch's worth was found hiding in the same pool.** After batch 14's 10 are set aside, the "ready to verify and build today" own-controller low-risk pool is close to exhausted. Any batch 15 will very likely need either (a) the dedicated ~85-screen shared-controller sweep this entry and entry 140 both recommend (to determine how many of the "dead" `MasterController` screens are actually alive), or (b) an owner decision to start drawing from the clinical/billing-tier pool instead of continuing to stretch the shrinking administrative-tier pool.

**Research limitation:** no authenticated legacy browser session was available this pass (the only open tab was on the Angular rebuild dev server; navigating to a legacy screen redirected to `/#/login`). Everything in this entry is static/source verification only — backend-existence confirmation with a live session remains a standing Step 0 requirement before any of these screens enter rebuild.

**Action taken:** findings logged, nothing selected or built. Batch 14's proposed 10-screen list is presented to the owner for review; no code touched.

## 143. Batch 14 kickoff — individual verification of the 2 parked-list screens in the proposed 10, owner decision on final composition

**Context:** batch 14's proposed 10 included 2 screens present on `docs/ANGULAR_PARKED_SCREENS.md` (unresolved clinical-keyword hits) — `wardStoreMapper` ("ward") and `aiButtonConfiguration` ("doctor"). Per standing anti-drift discipline, neither can be selected off that list without individual verification first. Both were read at the source level (controller + real backend endpoint chain) before batch 14 started.

**`wardStoreMapper` — verified FALSE POSITIVE, low-risk.** `wardStoreMapperController.js` maps pharmacy/inventory stores to wards for stock-dispensing routing (`t_ward_store_mapper`: `ward_id`, `store_id`, `org_id`, `unit_id`, `status`) — pure asset/location CRUD, no clinical event data. Endpoints confirmed: `getWardStoremappedListPagination(Count)`, `getActiveWardMasterList` (ward *master* list, not patient-ward assignment), `saveWardStoremapper`, `activeInactiveWardStoreMapper`, `getWardStoreMapperById`, `updateWardStoremapper`. **Selected into batch 14.**

**`aiButtonConfiguration` — verified FALSE POSITIVE on the clinical-keyword hit itself, but binds real doctor-identity records.** Traced `/api/global/getDoctor` to `UserRoleMasterController.getDoctor()` (`legacy/Global/src/main/java/com/param/global/unit/controller/UserRoleMasterController.java:188-197`) → `iUserRoleMasterService.getDoctorMasterByUnitId()` → `DoctorMasterDto` (doctor master/employee record: name, specialty, DOB, mobile, email, ID docs — no patient/encounter/diagnosis/vitals fields). The screen itself just binds an AI-assist button config to a doctor-or-speciality via this lookup — config metadata, not a clinical workflow. Not a mistag in the clinical-data sense, but it's the one screen in this batch that reaches into a named-individual-practitioner entity rather than a pure aggregate/location lookup.

**Owner decision:** keep `wardStoreMapper` in batch 14 as verified; defer `aiButtonConfiguration` out of caution given the doctor-identity binding, rather than treat the false-positive read as fully dispositive. Not swapped for a replacement — entry 142 already confirmed no pre-vetted candidate remains in the screened pool, and manufacturing a rushed 10th pick was judged worse than a 9-screen batch. **Batch 14 final composition: 9 screens** — `wardStoreMapper`, `addfixedAssetMaster`, `assetReturn` + `detailsAssetReturn` (paired), `detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceWorklist`, `smsConfiguration`, `pharmacyformulationTypeMaster`, `dashboard`, `detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceScheduleWorklist`.

**Also carried into Step 0 from entry 142:** `wardStoreMapper` has a known live bug — `$scope.wardStoremappedList()` is called in save/update/status-toggle success handlers (ctrl:221,261,351) but only defined in commented-out code (ctrl:169-189), producing a live `ReferenceError` right after every successful save/update/status-toggle today. To be faithfully reproduced, not silently fixed, per standing parity policy. `detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceScheduleWorklist`'s cookie-dereference-before-login-guard bug (entry 142) will be independently re-checked against the recurring "unguarded dereference before login check" pattern (`assigningPickerDispenser`/`organizationMaster`/`assetCategoryMaster`) during this batch's own Step 0, not assumed from entry 142's tentative `roomModality`/entry-9 comparison.

**Action taken:** both screens individually verified; `aiButtonConfiguration` removed from batch 14 per owner decision (still parked, now with a resolved verification note rather than an open question — see `ANGULAR_PARKED_SCREENS.md` update). `wardStoreMapper` removed from the parked list, selected into batch 14. No code touched yet.

## 144. Phase 4 batch 14 — Step 0/1 audit findings across all 9 screens (`wardStoreMapper`, `addfixedAssetMaster`, `assetReturn`+`detailsAssetReturn`, `detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceWorklist`, `smsConfiguration`, `pharmacyformulationTypeMaster`, `dashboard`, `detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceScheduleWorklist`) — logged, not fixed except where noted

**Context:** consolidated findings from each screen's full Step 0 (confirm-real, backend-existence-with-live-session) + Step 1 (sharpened-checklist audit) pass. Every one of the roughly 25 real backend endpoints traced across all 9 screens resolved to a concrete, implemented controller→service→DAO chain — **no `modalityTypeMaster`/entry-67-style dead backend found anywhere in this batch.** One adjacent, out-of-scope finding surfaced during `smsConfiguration`'s audit was independently re-verified against the live dev DB and is logged separately below (not a `smsConfiguration` bug itself).

**`wardStoreMapper`:**
- **Confirmed live bug (already known, entry 142):** `$scope.wardStoremappedList()` is called in save/update/status-toggle success handlers (ctrl:221,261,351) but only defined in a commented-out block (ctrl:169-189) — a live `ReferenceError` right after every successful save/update/status-toggle toast. Practical effect: the table is never actually refreshed after any of these three actions (the real refresh path, `initMasterList`, is also never re-invoked in these handlers). Faithfully reproduce (do not silently add a real refresh) — fixing this is a behavioral change requiring sign-off.
- **New bug found:** `updateWardStoremapper`'s duplicate-check query (`DepartmentStoreMapperDaoImpl.java:390-391`) checks the wrong table (`t_dept_store_mapper`, the sibling Department flow) instead of `t_ward_store_mapper` — the "already mapped" guard on update is effectively non-functional for wards. Legacy Java bug, out of scope for this rebuild batch to fix; faithfully reproduce (i.e. don't invent a working duplicate-guard on the Angular side that legacy doesn't have).
- **New bug found:** `saveWardStoremapper`'s duplicate-check query (`DepartmentStoreMapperDaoImpl.java:296-301`) binds a `ward_id` parameter but compares it against the surrogate PK column (`dsm.ward_store_mapper_id`), not the real `ward_id` column — semantically wrong uniqueness check. Same treatment as above.
- **Categorization ambiguity, flagged rather than resolved:** the template lives under a folder literally named `assetManagement`, but the seeded privilege hierarchy places module id 860 ("Ward Store Mapper") under parent 661 "User Managment" → 531 "Unit Master", **not** under module 203 "Asset Management". No route-level gate exists in legacy at all (module 860 is nav-visibility-only) — per the established `department-store-mapper` sibling precedent, the rebuild route should carry `canActivate: [moduleAccessGuard]` with **no** `moduleId` in `data`, matching legacy's own lack of a resolve-block gate.
- Two duplicate, byte-identical `WardStoreMapper` entity classes exist (`Entity`/`Pharmacy` modules) — only the `Entity` one is actually used; the other is dead/orphaned, informational only.

**`addfixedAssetMaster`:**
- **Naming trap, worth flagging prominently:** despite the name, this is a search/list/bulk-approve/cancel screen ("Add Fixed Asset Opening Balance"), not a create form — the actual create/edit form is a separate, much larger, not-yet-built screen (`addNewfixedAssetMaster`, legacy state, 1664-line controller). The original batch plan's premise that `new/src/app/features/fixed-asset-master/` is this screen's list-companion was wrong — that's an unrelated Asset-Type/Category master. The real already-built companion is `details-fixed-asset-master/`, whose Back button already points (currently dead) at `/addfixedAssetMaster`.
- **Confirmed need for the "not yet available" toast pattern**, per this batch's plan: both the "Add New" button and the Edit action target the not-yet-built `addNewfixedAssetMaster` and need a placeholder toast. The "Details" action is a real link to the already-built `details-fixed-asset-master` and should be wired for real via the existing `commonDetailService`/handoff pattern.
- **Bug to faithfully reproduce:** `isEdit()`/`isCancel()` have no final `else` branch in their status if/else chain — any status value outside `{Pending, New, Closed, Cancelled}` implicitly renders the Edit/Cancel icons as *enabled* rather than disabled. Legacy gap, port as-is.
- `rejectReasonService`'s result is fetched on every load but never consumed anywhere in the template — real endpoint, dead consumer. Harmless; faithfully fire-and-ignore, or disclose if omitted.

**`assetReturn` + `detailsAssetReturn` (paired):**
- **Confirmed real, live two-step workflow**, not just similarly-named features: this pair (the "initiating return" half) hands off via shared DB status values (id 2 "Pending", id 4 "Completed" on `t_asset_return`/`t_asset_return_detail`) into the already-built `asset-return-acceptance`/`asset-return-acceptance-details` pair (the "receiving store accepts" half) — no direct UI link, the "hop" is purely status-driven polling of the same table by two independent screens. Worth surfacing as a documented integration point (shared status enum) rather than duplicated magic-string lookups, at Step 3's normal discretion.
- Handoff between `assetReturn` → `detailsAssetReturn` uses the same in-memory `commonDetailService` singleton pattern as the already-built acceptance pair (`CommonDetailHandoffService`) — reuse directly, no new mechanism needed.
- **Bug to faithfully reproduce:** the Print action references an undefined variable (`REPORT_API_URI1`) — throws immediately on click, currently fully broken in legacy.
- **Bug to faithfully reproduce:** the results table's "Returning Store"/"Accepting Store" column headers, sort keys, and displayed fields are cross-wired relative to each other (confirmed via source, not just a suspicion) — functionally works (search/sort/display all still resolve to *some* value) but the human-facing labels don't match what a naive reading of the field names would suggest. Get the real field mapping right by testing against real data in Step 5, not by guessing from names.
- **Bug to faithfully reproduce:** the Cancel modal's Save button is gated on `cancelNote` being non-empty, but the actual validation function checks `cancelReason` instead — a user with a reason but no note is incorrectly blocked.
- `{{item.type}}` column has no backing DTO field — always renders blank, dead/unmapped; omit rather than build as if live.
- `addNewAssetReturnController.js` (the create/edit form for this same list) is a separate, substantial, not-yet-built screen — explicitly out of this batch's scope, not bundled in.

**`detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceWorklist`:**
- Confirmed genuinely distinct from the already-built `details-corrective-maintenance` (different parent list, different save endpoint, narrower allocation-only field set) — no duplicate-build risk.
- **Real gap in already-built code, needs fixing as part of this batch's wiring (not a sign-off item — this is the expected "graduate the stub" step):** `corrective-maintenance-worklist.ts`'s `goToDetails()` currently discards the clicked row entirely and navigates to a route that doesn't exist yet in `app.routes.ts`. This batch needs to: add the route, create a dedicated handoff service (mirroring the existing `CorrectiveMaintenanceHandoffService`/`DetailScheduleWorklistHandoffService` pattern), and fix `goToDetails()` to actually forward the row.
- Confirmed dead, copy-pasted markup in this screen's own template (PMS/Accessories/Checklist modals, zero trigger buttons, zero controller support) — inert residue from the sibling `details-corrective-maintenance` screen; omit from the rebuild rather than wire up as functional.
- **Bugs to faithfully reproduce:** save always hardcodes `maintenanceWorklistStatusId: 3` ("assigned") regardless of prior status; the edit-lock only checks status 2/4, leaving Cancelled/Reopen rows editable.

**`smsConfiguration`:** clean screen itself (real, fully wired, no dead backend, module-774 gate confirmed). Two bugs to faithfully reproduce: a case-typo (`isSmsSend` vs `isSMSSend`) makes a "default to SMS if nothing selected" safety net dead code; `clearMobileTempFields` is defined twice, the second definition silently wins and runs the wrong tab's clear logic.

**Adjacent finding, NOT part of `smsConfiguration` itself — real credentials currently sitting in this dev DB, blast radius not yet fully mapped:**
- `t_unit_application_billing_configuration.smtp_user_pwd` currently holds a real plaintext password (`Cht@12345` for `info@clinivantage.co.in`, confirmed via direct query against this dev DB, unit_id=1).
- `SmsUtilityConfigDaoImpl.getEmailUtilityByTriggerId`/`getEmailBulkUtilityByTriggerId` (`legacy/Global/.../dao/SmsUtilityConfigDaoImpl.java:639,901`) select this value into `mailPassword` in plaintext. These two methods have **no direct `@RequestMapping`** of their own — they're called internally from ~50 other controllers/services purely to trigger sending an email; I did not confirm whether any of those ~50 call sites forward the returned value back into an HTTP response, which would be needed to make this a client-facing disclosure like the already-fixed SMTP/Razorpay finding.
- Separately, `SmsUtility.java` hardcodes a fallback third-party SMS-gateway credential in source (`user=clinivantage&pass=1234562018&sender=CHTHOS`, 9+ call sites) used whenever the DB's `sender_id` is null — a hardcoded-secret-in-source issue (server-side use only, not returned to any client), lower severity than the SMTP password above.
- The one endpoint in this area that **is** directly client-reachable (`POST /getSmsUtilityByTriggerIdForInPatientCheckInForm` → `getSmsUtilityByTriggerId`) only returns `sender_id`, not `smtp_user_pwd` — and `sender_id` is currently NULL in this dev DB, so nothing is actively leaking through that specific path today.
- **Blast-radius investigation now complete (owner-requested, done before continuing batch 14):** all ~64 internal call sites of `getEmailUtilityByTriggerId`/`getEmailBulkUtilityByTriggerId` across ADT, LIS, Global/OPD, and Billing were individually traced. **Verdict: not a live, active client-facing leak today.** 41 of 64 sites discard the return value entirely (bare statement, no assignment). **12 sites (11 in LIS — `BiochemReportReleaseController`×3, `BioChemResultEntryController`×2, `ReportHandOverServiceImpl`, `OutSourceMasterServiceImpl`, `SerologyResultController`×4 — plus 1 in `RISWorkbenchServiceImpl`) do forward a field of the email-trigger `Response` into their own enclosing `@RequestMapping` method's HTTP response** — but in every one of these 12, the field actually copied is `.getMessage()`, which in the current code is always either `null` or a fixed literal string ("Failed To Send Mail") — **never** the DTO/`mailPassword`/`sender_id` data, which lives only in `.getListObject()`/`.getObject()` and is never read at any of these 12 sites. So today, nothing leaks.
  - **However, this is a latent, one-edit-away risk, not a non-issue:** `SmsUtilityConfigServiceImpl.getEmailUtilityByTriggerId` returns the DAO's raw `Response` object unchanged (aside from occasionally overwriting `.getMessage()`) — it never sanitizes/nulls out `.getListObject()`/`.getObject()` before returning. The forwarding wiring at all 12 sites is already live; the only thing preventing a real leak is that `.getMessage()` happens to never be populated with anything sensitive today. A future edit adding "more helpful" diagnostic detail to any of these 12 methods (a normal-looking, plausible change, not an exotic one) would leak the plaintext SMTP password with zero additional review needed at the call site itself.
  - **Recommended hardening** (not yet applied, needs owner approval — legacy Java code, own dedicated branch per `CLAUDE.md`'s Maker-Checker rule, out of scope for the Angular rebuild): have `getEmailUtilityByTriggerId`/`getEmailBulkUtilityByTriggerId` null out `listObject`/`object` on the `Response` they return (or return a stripped copy), the same defense-in-depth pattern as the already-applied `stripClientFacingSecrets()` fix, applied at the point closest to the actual secret rather than relying on all 12+ downstream call sites individually staying careful forever.
  - **Unrelated bug found during this trace, logged for awareness:** `BiochemReportReleaseController.java`'s `releaseMailOnCreditNote` (~line 821) has an uninitialized-variable NPE — the local `response` object it tries to attach data to is declared `null` and never assigned before use, so this code path throws and never successfully returns. Unrelated to the security question; not fixed here.
- **Not escalated to a DECISION_NEEDED doc** — confirmed not a live/active exploit (unlike JWT/resetPassword), so it doesn't meet that bar. Logged here in full for owner awareness and a future hardening pass.

**`pharmacyformulationTypeMaster`:** confirmed genuinely distinct from 2 similarly-named sibling screens (`formulationTypeROAMaster`, `formulationTypeToDosageTypeMapperController`) — no duplicate-build risk. Real, fully wired, module-459 gate confirmed. Findings:
- **Bug to faithfully reproduce:** the server-side "in use, can't deactivate" guard (checks active Item/FormulationAndRoa references) throws correctly, but the controller's error-handling expects HTTP 406 while the actual failure is a generic 500 — so the specific "assigned to Items/ROA" message never reaches the user (they see a generic error instead), *and* the status toggle's checkbox visually stays flipped-off until the next list reload, out of sync with the DB (which never actually changed). Faithfully reproduce both symptoms, or flag for owner sign-off if fixing.
- The "Clinical Unit" input field has no `ng-model` at all — fully decorative/dead in legacy (the backend does return a real `unit` value on single-record fetch, but the frontend never reads it into the model). Recommend dropping this dead field from the rebuild per established "confirmed-dead → omit" convention, same treatment as other batches' inert UI.
- The update flow unconditionally sends `status: true` regardless of a record's actual current status — currently harmless only because the Edit icon is disabled whenever a record is inactive; faithfully reproduce the coupling, don't treat the always-`true` payload as safe in isolation.

**`dashboard` (CEO dashboard):** confirmed real, fully wired, no route-level module gate (matches `home`/`billingGroupMaster` precedent — only per-tile `checkAccess()` gates on the 6 Quick Links). No nurseDashboard-style external AI/Supabase integration risk found. Bugs to faithfully reproduce:
- The "Finance" panel's 5th chart targets a `&lt;canvas id="bar2"&gt;` that is commented out in the template — this widget silently fails to render on every load (console-only error), never visibly broken to a user but genuinely non-functional today.
- The "Administration" panel's 2nd chart is wired to the same data variables (`data5`/`labels5`/`options5`) as the separate "Visits" pie-chart logic — the panel titled "Administration" actually displays Visits data, not administration data.
- Two entire dead-code branches (`$scope.click`'s first definition + `backToThePage()`) are silently overwritten before any template binding reads them — confirmed zero template references, safe to omit entirely from the rebuild.

**`detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceScheduleWorklist`:**
- **Bug-pattern classification (this batch's specific request), resolved:** the live `cookieObjectAppConfig.assetMaintenanceSpecialityId` dereference-before-login-guard crash (ctrl:20-26) is **NOT** the same shape as the named `assigningPickerDispenser`/`organizationMaster`/`assetCategoryMaster` family — those three's current source has the guard running correctly *before* any cookie dereference (their actual, already-fixed bugs were a missing-`$state`-DI crash and an unrelated `selectedTopStore` null-deref, not a guard-ordering issue). This screen's bug is instead a fresh recurrence of the **already-catalogued entry-17 pattern** (`preventiveScheduleWorklistController.js`/`pmScheduleController.js`, already fixed there via a proper `if(cookieObjectAppConfig == undefined){ $state.go('applicationConfiguration'); return; }` guard) — same 2-line snippet, just never remediated in this particular file. **Per the established entry-17/batch-13 precedent, the Angular rebuild uses the safe guard-then-read pattern directly (narrowly-scoped, unambiguous rebuild fix, no sign-off needed) — legacy itself is left untouched.**
- **Real gap in already-built code, needs fixing as part of this batch's wiring:** `corrective-maintenance-schedule-worklist.ts`'s `goToDetails()` has the same discard-the-row-and-navigate-to-a-nonexistent-route gap as its sibling worklist screen above — same fix needed (route + handoff service + forward the row).
- **Bug/gap to disclose:** `saveReplace()` builds a client-side object but never persists it (no `GenericService` call at all) — the Replace modal's Save button currently does nothing durable. The Return button is fully commented out in the template (unreachable). Both faithfully non-functional today; flag rather than silently complete them.
- Two different status-ID spaces (`maintenanceWorklistStatusId` vs `maintenanceScheduleStatusId`) share overlapping numeric values that mean different things in each (`4` = "assigned" in one's comment, "Work In Progress" in the other) — keep them as two separate vocabularies in the rebuild, do not share a lookup/enum between them.

**Action taken:** all of the above are either logged for the owner's general awareness (legacy bugs unrelated to this rebuild's own correctness, or the SMS/SMTP credential item needing its own separate investigation) or fall within Step 3's normal rebuild discretion (dead/inert UI ported as dead/inert, the two "graduate the stub" wiring fixes on already-built batch-13 screens, the entry-17-pattern defensive guard fix). The `wardStoreMapper` categorization ambiguity and the `assetReturn` table label-mapping question are flagged for explicit attention during Step 3/5 rather than silently resolved one way. No DECISION_NEEDED doc opened this pass — nothing here rises to the confirmed-severity-1 bar the JWT/resetPassword findings did.

## 145. Phase 4 batch 14 — Step 4/5 (golden-trace re-audit + live interaction testing) results across all 9 screens; 2 real rebuild bugs found and fixed, 2 real pre-existing legacy backend bugs found (not fixed, logged)

**Context:** real authenticated-session testing against the live dev backend (`ng serve --proxy-config proxy.conf.json`, same-origin proxy to `localhost:8080`, real `cookieObject` session shared across both origins). Every screen navigated directly at its real Angular route (not a hash fragment — confirmed early that this rebuild uses real path-based routing, unlike legacy's hash-based scheme). Console and network traffic inspected on each screen; two of the four "graduate the stub" handoffs (`detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceWorklist`, `detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceScheduleWorklist`) were exercised via a synthetic row object dispatched directly through the live component instance (`window.ng.getComponent(...)`, then calling `goToDetails()` with fabricated data) rather than a real row click, since this dev DB currently has zero seed rows for both corrective-maintenance worklists — same class of data-availability gap this project has hit repeatedly (`PMRequest`, `insuranceMaster`, etc.), not a rebuild defect.

**`dashboard`** — PASS. Real data confirmed for all 4 panels; both faithfully-reproduced bugs visually confirmed live: the Finance panel's 5th chart is correctly omitted (matches legacy's dead commented-out canvas), and the "Administration" panel's second chart is confirmed rendering under the literal title "Visits" (the mislabeling is real and visible, not just present in source). Zero console errors.

**`wardStoreMapper`** — PASS. Layout matches legacy exactly. Store dropdown returns zero real options — independently confirmed via the same JS query run against the LIVE LEGACY app (`localhost:8080`) that its own Store dropdown is equally empty in this dev DB (a genuine data-seeding gap, not a rebuild bug). Ward dropdown correctly shows real seeded data ("Test Ward"). Zero console errors.

**`addfixedAssetMaster`** — PASS. Layout matches legacy exactly (filters, table columns). "Add New" button's "not yet available" toast confirmed firing correctly live.

**`assetReturn` / `detailsAssetReturn`** — PASS on layout and graceful-degradation; 2 real pre-existing legacy backend bugs found (see below). "Add New" not-yet-available toast confirmed firing.

**`pharmacyformulationTypeMaster`** — PASS, full round-trip verified: typed a real Code+Type, clicked Save, confirmed the "Are you sure you want to Save?" modal, clicked Ok, got a real `Formulation Type Inserted Successfully` toast, and the new row appeared in the grid with a real status toggle — genuine create flow working end-to-end against the real backend, not just a rendered form. Status toggle (deactivate) also confirmed working for a non-in-use record.

**`smsConfiguration`** — Initially FAILED live (a real 404 on load, visible "Error" toast) — see bug #1 below, now fixed and re-verified clean.

**`detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceWorklist`** (+ `corrective-maintenance-worklist.ts` fix) — PASS. Synthetic-row test confirmed: clicking the (simulated) eye icon navigates to the new route and the handed-off row's data (asset code/name, request type/criticality) renders correctly in the Asset Details section. Zero console errors.
