**`detailsCorrectiveMaintenanceScheduleWorklist`** (+ `corrective-maintenance-schedule-worklist.ts` fix) — Initially FAILED a sub-check live (a literal `"undefined"` string rendered in the Resolution Expected By field) — see bug #2 below, now fixed and re-verified clean. Handoff/navigation itself confirmed working via the same synthetic-row technique.

---

### Bugs found live, fixed directly (rebuild-implementation bugs, not parity questions — per established project precedent, fixed without holding for approval)

**1. `sms-configuration.service.ts` called every endpoint with an incorrect `/api/globals` prefix.** `SmsUtilityConfigController.java` has **no class-level `@RequestMapping`** at all (confirmed by reading the file top to bottom) — its real paths are bare (e.g. `/sms-utility-config/{orgId}/{unitId}`, `/trigger-master`), matching legacy's own frontend construction (`BASE_URL + ROOT_URL + "/sms-utility-config/..."`, where `ROOT_URL` is the empty string, `url.config.js:19`). The original Step 0 audit spec for this screen wrongly assumed a shared `/api/globals` base — true for sibling controllers like `DepartmentStoreMapperController`, not this one. This caused a live 404 on every endpoint at initial page load. Fixed all 8 endpoint paths in `sms-configuration.service.ts`; added the corresponding bare-path proxy rules to `new/proxy.conf.example.json` (and the local, gitignored `proxy.conf.json`) so local dev testing can reach them. Re-verified live: all endpoints now return 200, no error toast, clean console.

**2. `details-corrective-maintenance-schedule-worklist.html` used `[value]=` bindings on 6 disabled `<input>` fields with no fallback for `undefined`.** Angular's property binding on a native `<input>`'s `value` coerces `undefined` to the literal string `"undefined"` (a real browser/DOM quirk — `element.value = undefined` stringifies — unlike a safe `{{ }}` interpolation elsewhere in the same file, which renders nothing for `undefined`). Observed live: the "Resolution Expected By" field displayed the text `undefined` when the synthetic test row didn't set that property. Added `|| ''` to all 6 affected bindings (`resolutionExpectedDateTime`, `vendorObj().vendorName`, `spareStock()`, `detail().assetcategory`, `detail().assetCode`, `detail().assetName` — the last three inside the Replace modal, not visible without opening it, same latent bug). Re-verified live: field now renders empty as expected.

### Real, pre-existing legacy backend bugs found live (NOT fixed — legacy Java, own dedicated branch/approval needed per `CLAUDE.md`'s Maker-Checker rule, out of scope for this Angular batch)

**3. `assetReturn`'s dropdowns endpoint (`GET /api/asset/management/asset/return/dropdowns/{unitId}/{orgId}/{userId}`) throws a live 500 for real, valid input (`unitId=1`).** Root cause confirmed via the real Tomcat `catalina.out` stack trace: `AssetReturnService.getDropdowns()` calls the shared `GlobalDao.getStoreList(unitId)` (`GlobalDao.java:785-793`), whose HQL (`SELECT store FROM Store AS store WHERE ... ORDER BY store.storeName`) throws `org.hibernate.exception.SQLGrammarException: could not extract ResultSet` at the JDBC layer. The `m_store` table and its expected columns were confirmed to exist and look structurally correct via direct `psql` inspection — root cause not fully isolated (would need deeper Hibernate/Postgres-dialect investigation), but definitively confirmed real, live, and reproducible, not an artifact of the rebuild's own request construction (path/argument order double-checked against the real `@RequestMapping`, confirmed correct). The rebuild degrades gracefully (loader stops, dropdowns render empty, no crash, no visible error) — same defensive `error: () => loader.stop()` pattern used elsewhere in this rebuild.

**4. `assetReturn`'s details endpoint (`GET /api/asset/management/asset/return/{unitId}/{orgId}/{id}`) throws a live 500 (NPE) when `id` doesn't correspond to a real row.** Confirmed via stack trace: `AssetReturnService.getAssetReturnDetails()` (`AssetReturnService.java:238`) calls `.getId()` on the fetched entity with no null-check — a real, unguarded dereference when the row genuinely doesn't exist (e.g. a stale/direct deep-link, or this batch's synthetic id=0 test with no real seed data). Same class of gap as `PMRequest`'s already-logged live 500 on `VendorController` (entry 138) — a real, currently-broken legacy backend call, independent of this rebuild, flagged for the owner's awareness. The rebuild degrades gracefully here too.

**Action taken:** bugs #1 and #2 fixed directly and re-verified live (commit on `rebuild/phase4-batch14`). Bugs #3 and #4 logged for owner awareness only — legacy Java, unrelated to this rebuild's correctness, no code touched. All 9 screens confirmed working (or gracefully degrading against known-broken legacy backend calls) via real browser interaction against the real backend. Batch held on `rebuild/phase4-batch14`, not merged, not pushed, per standing instruction.

## 146. Batch 15 scoping research — MasterController cluster sample (37/70, 53%): 0% alive, 94.6% dead-mockup, 5.4% ambiguous — recommend the clinical/billing-tier pool instead

**Context:** owner-requested scoped estimation pass (explicitly NOT a full Step 0-7 audit, no code built or touched) to inform the batch 15 (a)/(b) decision flagged in entries 142/144. First recomputed the actual population precisely rather than relying on prior rough estimates: filtering `tools/angular-audit/data/states_enriched.json` for `controller == "MasterController"` AND `clinical_risk == "no"` AND `billing_risk == "no"`, then excluding anything already built (cross-checked against `new/src/app/app.routes.ts`), yields **70 screens**, not the "~85-90" figure loosely cited before — this is a real recount, not a new discrepancy to chase. Breakdown: Linen & Laundry 38, Common/Shared 17, Admin - Org/Unit Master 5, Asset Management 3, ADT - Bed Management 2, and 5 module-singletons.

**Sample:** 37 of 70 (53%), stratified across every represented module (14 of 38 Linen & Laundry, 8 of 17 Common/Shared, all 5 Admin-Org/Unit Master, all 3 Asset Management, both ADT-Bed Management, all 5 singletons), split across 3 independent research passes. Each screen individually confirmed against the real current template file (not assumed from name/module) plus a real grep of `common-controller.js` for that exact state name, per the same real-bindings discipline every prior batch has used.

**Structural fact, confirmed across all 3 passes independently:** `common-controller.js` (463 lines) has **zero per-state branching logic** — no `$state.current.name`/`switch` on state anywhere in the file, and none of the 37 sampled state names appear in it even once. It never defines `vm` or uses `controllerAs`, and its only real logic is a generic `$rootScope.$on('customEvent', ...)` DOM-text-swapper keyed on unrelated lab/billing master-page labels, plus generic tab-toggle/datepicker helpers. This structurally confirms why so much of this cluster is dead: the shared controller genuinely has nothing screen-specific to give any of these templates.

**Result: 0 ALIVE, 35 DEAD-MOCKUP, 2 AMBIGUOUS (0% / 94.6% / 5.4%)** — worse than the prior ~83%-dead partial-sample estimate, not better. Neither "ambiguous" case is a real alive candidate on inspection: `appointments` is a bare tab-navigation shell with no content of its own (its aliveness is entirely deferred to unsampled nested child states); `MPShahPatientList` is broken for a different reason (a correct `ng-repeat` exists in source but is commented out, and the live row has none, so its `$rootScope`-populated data can never render, plus its own `searchPatient()` doesn't exist) — not a case of "close to alive," a differently-broken dead end.

**Notable sub-finding, not part of the alive/dead tally but worth flagging for later:** at least 4 sampled screens (`newAdmissionRequest`, `procedureConfig`, and the `sendToCondemnation`/`staffExchangeLinen`/`issueDirtyLinen` trio) aren't simple static mockups — they carry fully real-looking, well-formed `ng-model`/`ng-repeat`/`ng-click` bindings that are verbatim copies of a genuinely-built, separately-wired sibling controller's markup (`admissionRequestController.js`, `diagnosticNonSurgicalProcedureConfigController.js`, and an unidentified "Store Indent" screen respectively) — but the route table wires these specific states to generic `MasterController` instead of their real controller, permanently orphaning the copied bindings. This suggests some of this cluster is decoy/duplicate routing onto already-real functionality living elsewhere in the app under a different state name, not a "half-built screen" — a distinct maintenance question (dead-route cleanup) from "is there real business logic to rebuild here," and out of scope for this research pass.

**Recommendation for batch 15, evidence-based per the owner's own stated bar (15+ alive screens needed to justify characterizing this cluster as batch 15's target population):** **0 of 37 sampled screens are alive.** This does not meet the bar by a wide margin — recommend **starting batch 15 from the clinical/billing-tier risk pool instead**, not this cluster. Per the owner's own framing, this is a real, first deliberate policy shift past the "easy" administrative tier and will require elevated scrutiny going forward: tighter parity requirements (clinical/financial correctness has direct patient-safety and billing-accuracy stakes, unlike an inert admin mockup), and likely domain-expert involvement for screens this project's own agents can't fully validate from code-reading alone (e.g. clinical workflow correctness, billing calculation edge cases) — a different risk profile from every batch this project has run so far.

**Action taken:** research only, no screens selected, no code built or touched, matching the owner's explicit scope for this pass. Batch 15's actual screen list is NOT proposed here — held for the owner's own (a)/(b) decision.

## 147. Decoy-routing finding, split out from entry 146 — 5 screens wired to `MasterController` are orphaned copies of real, working sibling functionality, not abandoned code

**Context:** while sampling the MasterController cluster for entry 146, 5 of the 35 confirmed-dead screens turned out to be a meaningfully different case than the other 30 (which are genuine static/hardcoded mockups with no real functionality behind them anywhere). These 5 carry fully real, well-formed `ng-model`/`ng-repeat`/`ng-click` bindings — not decorative, not orphaned-by-typo — that are verbatim copies of a **separate, genuinely-built, separately-wired sibling controller's markup**. The functionality these bindings call for is real and exists in the codebase today; it's just unreachable from these 5 specific routes because the route table (`app.js`) points them at generic `MasterController` (which defines none of the referenced scope variables) instead of the real controller that actually implements them.

**The 5 screens and their real, working counterparts:**
- `newAdmissionRequest` — bindings (`admissionRequest.*`, `specialityList`/`doctorList`/`reasonList`/`bedCategoryList`, `getDoctorBySpeciality(...)`) match `admissionRequestController.js`, which is real, separately wired, and presumably reachable via its own (different) legacy state/route.
- `procedureConfig` — bindings (`configObj.*`, `specialityList`, `diagnosticProcConfigList`, `save()`/`update()`/`showUpdateBtn()`) match `diagnosticNonSurgicalProcedureConfigController.js`, likewise real and separately wired.
- `sendToCondemnation`, `staffExchangeLinen`, `issueDirtyLinen` (3 of the Linen & Laundry sample) — all three carry the exact same `vm.model.fromStore/toStore/storeList/StoreIndentData()/CancelIndent()` boilerplate verbatim, matching what looks like a genuine "Store Indent" screen's real controller (not yet identified by name in this pass — would need a follow-up grep for `StoreIndentData`/`CancelIndent` across `legacy/HIS-WAR/.../controller/` to find the real owning controller and its own live route).

**What this means, and what it doesn't:** this is NOT "half-built code" or a rebuild opportunity in the usual sense — the real, presumably-functioning screen already exists elsewhere in the legacy app under a different route. It IS a real, live user-facing gap: any user who navigates to (or has bookmarked, or has in an old menu link) `#/newAdmissionRequest`, `#/procedureConfig`, `#/sendToCondemnation`, `#/staffExchangeLinen`, or `#/issueDirtyLinen` today lands on a screen that looks plausible (real-looking form fields) but silently does nothing — no error, no redirect, just permanently orphaned bindings that can never populate or save. A user unfamiliar with the "real" route would have no way to know the functionality they're looking for exists elsewhere under a different name/path.

**Flagged as a future decision point, NOT fixed now:** is this worth fixing in legacy (repointing these 5 routes' `controller:` to their real, working counterparts, or removing/redirecting the decoy routes entirely) — independent of, and prior to, whether any of these 5 specific screens individually gets selected into a future Angular rebuild batch? This is a legacy-side routing/menu-hygiene question, not an Angular rebuild question, and per `CLAUDE.md`'s Maker-Checker rule would need its own dedicated branch and owner sign-off if pursued. Not actioned here — logged and flagged only, per explicit instruction.

**Action taken:** logged for owner awareness and future decision; no code touched; not merged into the dead-mockup tally's implied "nothing to see here" read for these 5 screens specifically.

## 148. Batch 15 candidate research — first clinical/billing-tier batch, screened per the new Clinical/Billing-Tier Addendum

**Context:** real Step 0-level research (state/template/controller confirmed real, backend traced to a concrete Java controller→service→DAO chain, per this project's standing discipline) on 12 candidates — 6 billing-only, 6 clinical-only, all with their own dedicated controller (not `MasterController`) — deliberately favoring plain master-data/lookup screens to calibrate the new tier's process safely, per the addendum. For each candidate, explicitly classified as PURE-LOOKUP (Step 6a only) vs. CALCULATION-INPUT/SAFETY-ADJACENT (would require Step 6b, currently unavailable — no SME exists on this project yet).

**Billing-only (6 checked):**
- `identificationMaster`, `currencyMaster`, `currencydenomination`, `cardTypeMaster` — all confirmed real end-to-end, simple (2-6 fields), **PURE-LOOKUP**. One disclosure carried forward from `currencydenomination`: its entity has a dormant `ratePerDoller` column that is never set or read by this screen's actual save/update flow — rate-shaped data sitting unused, not a calculation the screen performs today; worth a future look, not a blocker.
- `corporateMasterGlobal` — real, but disqualified on two independent grounds: (1) a 1,565-line, multi-tab screen (company master + employee/next-of-kin sub-workflow + grade-mapping matrix + document upload) — not structurally simple; (2) stores `creditLimit`/`creditDays`, a real monetary cap/threshold — **CALCULATION-INPUT**, would require Step 6b. Flagged as a candidate for a *later*, more deliberate clinical/billing batch once an SME exists for 6b, not for this calibration batch.
- `tariffMaster` — real, and its own fields are technically pure-lookup (a named, date-ranged "tariff scheme" container with no rate field of its own), but it anchors 3 separate many-to-many rate-mapping sub-screens and is the parent FK for sibling screens (`unitServiceTariffMaster`, `serviceTariffMaster` — not yet audited) that likely do hold real rates. Flagged for extra scrutiny in a future batch, not picked now.

**Clinical-only (6 checked):**
- `deliveryTypeMaster`, `encounterTypeMaster`, `consentTypeMaster` — all confirmed real end-to-end, simple (2-3 fields), **PURE-MASTER/administrative** (classification/legal-consent flags, no dosing or patient-identification computation of their own, despite `encounterTypeId` being a widely-referenced FK downstream).
- `diagnosisMaster` — **confirmed DEAD BACKEND**, same defect class as `modalityTypeMaster`/entry 67, and specifically the same already-documented dead "homecare" cluster as entry 77 (`DIAGNOSIS`/`CAREPLAN` constants live in `homecare.url.config.js`, no Java `homecare` package exists anywhere in the tree). Also has an independent dead-button binding (`ng-click="updateCarePlan()"` calls a function the controller never defines). Recommend logging this as an additional member of entry 77's cluster (owner's call whether to formally append it there) — excluded from this batch, not a calibration candidate.
- `allergyTypeMaster` — real, working, deceptively simple-looking (3 fields) but **SAFETY-ADJACENT**: `allergyTypeId` is a real FK on `AllergyPatientMapper` (patient-specific allergy record surfaced via the coversheet allergy-alert icon) and on `AntibioticTestDoseDto` in the pharmacy nursing-workstation drug-administration module. Would require Step 6b (clinician sign-off) if selected — not picked for this calibration batch.
- `doseMaster` — real, working, the simplest-looking screen of all 12 (2 fields: code + description) but **the textbook disguised-safety-adjacent case the addendum itself describes**: `doseId` is the real FK populating dose selection on `MedicationModel`/medication orders. Would require Step 6b if selected — not picked for this calibration batch.

**Net result: 7 clean, confirmed-real, pure-lookup candidates for a 5-screen batch** (4 billing-only, 3 clinical-only), plus 2 real screens correctly screened OUT as safety-adjacent (`allergyTypeMaster`, `doseMaster` — good candidates for a *later* batch once 6b has an SME), 1 real screen screened out as too complex/calculation-bearing (`corporateMasterGlobal`), 1 real-but-anchoring-a-rate-cluster screen deferred (`tariffMaster`), and 1 confirmed dead backend (`diagnosisMaster`, folded into the existing entry-77 dead cluster).

**Action taken:** research only, no screens selected/built yet — final 5-screen selection and composition disclosure presented to the owner separately for explicit approval before Step 0-7 execution begins.

## 149. Phase 4 batch 15 — Step 0/1 audit findings across all 5 screens (`identificationMaster`, `cardTypeMaster`, `deliveryTypeMaster`, `encounterTypeMaster`, `consentTypeMaster`) — first batch under the Clinical/Billing-Tier Addendum

**Context:** deep Step 0/1 audits (going beyond entry 148's Step 0-level screening) on all 5 approved batch-15 screens. All 5 remain confirmed real end-to-end, backend traced to a concrete controller→service→DAO chain, no dead-backend gaps. A systemic observation across all 5: every screen uses the same asymmetric UI pattern — **Save routes through a two-click "Are you sure?" confirm modal, Update does not** (fires immediately on click) — confirmed identically in all 5 controllers. This is a real, consistent legacy convention, not a per-screen bug; reproduce it as-is across the batch rather than "fixing" it into consistency.

**`identificationMaster`:** real, backend-confirmed, 6-field form. **Severity-1 finding escalated separately and immediately, per owner instruction**, to `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_IdentificationMasterCreateFlagReset.md`: the CREATE flow's DAO unconditionally resets `isExpiryRequired`/`isNumerical`/`isAlphanumeric` to `'N'` after mapping the DTO, discarding whatever the user checked — invisible from the frontend alone, only found by reading the DAO layer directly. Screen is **on hold pending owner review of that doc** — Step 2+ not started for this screen. Also found: exact mutually-exclusive-flags rule (both checked = blocked client-side; neither checked = no validation, silently allowed), a real-but-unused `getActiveIdentificationList`/cache infrastructure this screen doesn't touch, and the standard "exits"-typo/empty-catch-block findings common across this batch.

**`cardTypeMaster`:** real, backend-confirmed, simplest field-count (2 fields), self-registered state confirmed with genuinely no module gate (matching the already-documented `billingGroupMaster`/`periodicityService` precedent — not a new gap). Has **real seed data** in this dev environment (6 real card types: Maestro, Diners Club, RuPay, American Express, Visa, Master Card) — the only screen in this batch with real rows to test against without fabricating data. `pinpadScheme` DTO field reconfirmed dead/unused on this screen (belongs to a different, unaudited sibling endpoint) — correctly excluded from the rebuild. Bugs to faithfully reproduce: status hardcoded to `'A'` on create (no create-as-inactive path), "exits" typo, no list-refresh after status toggle (relies on the checkbox's own binding), Update-mode UI flags (`saveBtnFlag`/`updateBtnFlag`) never reset after a successful update, and the client-side-only per-page search.

**`deliveryTypeMaster`:** real, backend-confirmed, simplest of the batch (2 fields, no pagination). Two confirmed, currently-live defects, both now resolved by owner decision:
- **Broken Edit flow**: the live template is missing its Update button entirely (only Angular's own `<!-- ngIf: updateBtnFlag -->` runtime comment remains — the original, correct button still exists commented-out nearby, and the real backend `/adt/updateDeliveryTypeMaster` endpoint works). Net effect: editing a row shows Save (not Update); same-name edits get rejected as duplicates, changed-name edits silently create a new row rather than updating the original.
- **Broken list load, confirmed live** (not just a static-analysis concern): after resolving the module-506 access gap (see `BR-local-dev-db-drift-role1-module506`) and refreshing the session, real network capture shows `GET /adt/getDeliveryTypeMaster` returning **405 Method Not Allowed** — the Java `@RequestMapping` is `POST`-only. This is not an environment-specific issue; the same mismatch would occur identically in any deployment. Combined with the separately-noted fully-commented-out sidebar menu path, this screen has apparently never displayed data for any real user.
- **Owner-approved: fix both directly in the rebuild** — restore a real Update flow using the existing, working backend endpoint, and call the list-load endpoint with the correct `POST` verb. Both are narrowly-scoped, unambiguous rebuild fixes (no legacy ambiguity about "what correct behavior looks like" — the backend contract is unambiguous in both cases), not held for further sign-off. The Angular rebuild will be the first version of this screen to actually display data. Also found: a validation-message copy-paste bug (Description's required-message is keyed off the Code field's own validity) and a silent DAO-layer 10-row cap with no UI to page further (the "Show entries"/"Search" controls are both dead — no wiring in the controller at all) — both faithfully reproduced as logged bugs, not fixed.

**`encounterTypeMaster`:** real, backend-confirmed, widely-referenced downstream (FK on `EncounterMaster`, `AdmittedPatientListDaoImpl`, `RadiologyMapper`, `DebitNoteMaster`, `LabSampleMasterDto`, `PatientRegistrationDaoImpl` — still just a classification lookup, no computation of its own). **Real, likely user-visible bug found**: the update-path duplicate-name-exclude-self query (`GET_ENCOUNTER_LIST_BY_NAME_NOT_ID`) has an HQL operator-precedence bug — `WHERE LOWER(name)=:x OR name=:x AND id=:excludeId` parses as `LOWER(name)=:x OR (name=:x AND id=:excludeId)`, so the id-exclusion only attaches to the second (redundant) branch. Practical effect: **editing a row without changing its name is likely to spuriously fail as "already exists"** far more often than the check was intended to allow. Also found: no uniqueness check on `encounterTypeCode` at all (name-only duplicate check), the create-path duplicate check is NOT org-scoped (global across all orgs) while the list itself is, status forced to `'A'` on create, and no UI rollback of the status toggle on a failed backend call. **Flagged for owner decision** on treatment (faithfully reproduce the spurious-failure behavior vs. propose a fix) — not yet decided, holding at the "log and flag" stage pending explicit direction, same as every other real backend defect this batch surfaced.

**`consentTypeMaster`:** real, backend-confirmed, an administrative/legal-consent-flag screen — `admissionConsent` confirmed to gate nothing else on the screen itself and to have no other downstream consumer in current code (only the row's own FK, `consentTypeMasterId`, is load-bearing elsewhere, matching entry 148's read). Bugs to faithfully reproduce: the same case-insensitive-duplicate-check defect as `encounterTypeMaster`'s create path but via a different mechanism (the bound parameter is never lowercased, only the column is — so an existing all-lowercase name won't catch a differently-cased duplicate), no code-uniqueness check, a fully-wired-but-commented-out RabbitMQ/OTIMS/EMR publish call on both create and update (no downstream sync fires today despite the code looking like it does — do not silently re-enable as part of this rebuild), and a wrong-message-on-not-found bug in the status-update path (currently invisible to users since the frontend ignores backend message text on that call). Also: a **new instance of the already-adjudicated entry-55 shared-`<modal>`-`cancel()`-undefined bug** (this screen wasn't part of that original 12-screen sweep) — per standing policy, safe to give this screen's `×` button ordinary working behavior without re-litigating, just noting the new instance here.

**Cross-cutting environment finding — resolved:** module 506 (`deliveryTypeMaster`'s gate) was not granted to the demo admin account used for live testing, confirmed via direct navigation — the other 4 screens' gates (388, 397, 399, and cardTypeMaster's no-gate) all worked normally for the same account. Owner-approved additive grant applied (`BR-local-dev-db-drift-role1-module506`), session refreshed via re-login, access confirmed working — which is what surfaced the live 405 finding above.

**Action taken:** 4 of 5 screens' Step 0/1 audits are clean-enough-to-proceed (with the noted bugs logged for faithful reproduction); `identificationMaster` is on hold pending the DECISION_NEEDED doc; `encounterTypeMaster`'s spurious-duplicate-check bug awaits an explicit owner decision on treatment; `deliveryTypeMaster`'s access gap awaits resolution before Step 5 can complete for that screen specifically. No screens merged or pushed; batch held on `rebuild/phase4-batch15`.

**Update:** owner decided `encounterTypeMaster`'s spurious-duplicate-check bug should be **faithfully reproduced**, not fixed — see entry 150 for live confirmation.

## 150. Phase 4 batch 15 — Step 4/5 (live browser-automation interaction testing) results across all 4 built screens; 3 real rebuild bugs found and fixed, 1 real cross-cutting bug corrected in scope, all confirmed bugs faithfully reproduced as designed

**Context:** real in-browser interaction testing (not golden-trace) against the live Java backend for `cardTypeMaster`, `deliveryTypeMaster`, `encounterTypeMaster`, `consentTypeMaster` — the 4 of 5 batch-15 screens actually built (`identificationMaster` remains held per entry 149, un-built, pending the DECISION_NEEDED doc). All 4 are now through Steps 0-5.

**`cardTypeMaster`:** create/update round-trip tested against real seed data (the "Maestro" row, id 6). Found and **fixed** a real severity-worthy crash: `requiredFieldsValid()`/`isCodeInvalid()`/`isNameInvalid()` called `.trim()` directly on form fields, and this dev DB's seed rows genuinely have `cardTypeCode: null` — editing any of them crashed the component (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'trim')`). Fixed with an `(value ?? '').trim()` null-guard; re-verified live (validation now correctly shows "Code is required" instead of crashing, full update round-trip succeeds once Code is filled in). The same build agent independently caught and corrected my own earlier mis-briefed claim that "Update never resets the form" was a bug — it verified `updateCardType()` genuinely does call `$scope.init()` on success, same as save — and instead surfaced a real, previously-undocumented one: the confirm modal's `popupArmed` signal is only re-armed by a *successful* save, so a **failed** save leaves it stuck disarmed, silently skipping the confirm modal on every subsequent Save click until one succeeds.

**Update, Step 6 review (2026-07-23):** the owner independently reproduced this exact behavior live during their side-by-side review and asked for it to be re-verified rather than taken on faith. Re-confirmed against the real legacy app (direct `popupArmed()`/`showConfirmModal()` signal reads at each step, not just source reading) that this is legacy's own genuine behavior, not a rebuild-introduced defect — `$scope.popUpFlag` has no reset in `saveCardType`'s error branch either. Given the choice to faithfully reproduce (as `encounterTypeMaster`'s HQL bug was) or fix, the owner chose **fix**: `doSave()`'s failure branch now also re-arms `popupArmed`, so the confirm modal correctly reappears before every save attempt, including right after a failure — only the confirm gate resets, form fields are left in place for correction, same as before. Verified live: success → duplicate rejection → modal correctly reappears on the next Save click → success. Committed on `rebuild/phase4-batch15`.

**`deliveryTypeMaster`:** both owner-approved fixes (entry 149) verified live, not just unit-tested. `POST /adt/getDeliveryTypeCount` and `POST /adt/getDeliveryTypeMaster` both confirmed 200 (previously 405) after the verb fix. Full create round-trip confirmed ("TST"/"Test Delivery Type" → real "Delivery Type Added Successfully...!!" toast → row appears). The restored Update flow, previously-dead search/page-size controls now genuinely functional, matching the established "restore obviously-intended-but-never-wired UI" precedent from prior batches.

**`encounterTypeMaster`:** full create/edit/update round-trip verified live (`POST /adt/saveEncounterTypeMaster`, `getEncounterTypeById`, `updateEncounterTypeMaster` all 200). Per the owner's explicit decision on entry 149, the HQL operator-precedence spurious-duplicate bug was **faithfully reproduced, not fixed** — confirmed live: editing a freshly-created row without changing its name genuinely triggers "Encounter type already exits...!!", exactly as the buggy HQL predicts.

**`consentTypeMaster`:** the only screen in this batch requiring real fixes to make the create flow work at all — found live, not from static review:
1. **All 6 endpoint paths had an erroneous `/api` prefix** (`/api/adt/getConsentTypeCount`, `/api/adt/saveConsentTypeMaster`, etc.) that 404'd against the real backend — `ConsentTypeMasterController`'s class-level `@RequestMapping` is `/adt`, no `/api` segment, confirmed by reading the legacy controller directly and cross-checking `encounterTypeMaster`/`deliveryTypeMaster`'s own services in this same batch (both correctly use bare `/adt/...`). The build agent's own doc comment had asserted the opposite ("deployment base `/api/adt/...`, matching every other sibling screen") — that claim was simply wrong; list-load silently fell back to "No Records Found" instead of surfacing an error, masking the failure completely. Fixed by removing the prefix from all 6 calls (`rebuild/phase4-batch15`, own commit).
2. **After fixing (1), saves still failed** with legacy's own generic "Consent type already exits...!!" toast despite the table having zero rows (confirmed via direct `psql` read before assuming anything) — traced via live `catalina.out`, not guessed: `adt.m_consent_type_master` was missing its `admission_consent` column entirely (`ConsentTypeMaster.java:91-92` maps it as a real field, the "Admission Consent" checkbox), so every insert threw a `PSQLException` silently swallowed by an empty `catch` block, indistinguishable from a real duplicate-name rejection by the frontend (legacy does the same collapsing — confirmed by reading `consentTypeMasterController.js:236-256`). Fixed via a single additive `ALTER TABLE`, logged as `BR-local-dev-db-drift-consenttype-admissionconsent` (committed to `main` directly, docs-only). Full create → real success toast → edit → update (correctly excludes self via `..._NOT_ID`, no false positive) → status toggle round-trip verified live after both fixes.

**Cross-cutting finding, corrected in scope during this batch's testing — global `.icon-edit`/`.icon-view` duplicate-glyph bug:** initially suspected (per the `AskUserQuestion` the owner approved "Fix now, own branch" for) to be a shared `styles.scss` bug affecting all 77 files using these classes. **Investigation before touching the CSS found this framing was wrong** — the shared `::after` rule is correct and load-bearing for ~73 files that use bare `icon-edit`/`icon-view` with no other icon-font class (it's their *only* glyph-rendering mechanism); deleting it as originally proposed would have broken icon rendering across most of the already-shipped rebuild. The real, narrow bug: exactly 4 templates redundantly combined a FontAwesome class (`fa fa-pencil`) *with* `icon-edit` on the same element, rendering FontAwesome's own glyph plus the shared `::after` glyph together — 3 pre-existing (`manufacturer-master`, `frequency-master`, `pharmacy-formulation-type-master`, fixed on `fix/global-icon-edit-view-duplicate-glyph`, pushed not merged, held for review) and 1 new to this batch (`delivery-type-master`, fixed directly on `rebuild/phase4-batch15`). `styles.scss` itself was not touched. Re-verified live via zoom screenshot on both `encounterTypeMaster` and `consentTypeMaster` post-fix: single clean pencil glyph, no duplication.

**Action taken:** all 4 built screens are now through Steps 0-5. 3 real rebuild bugs fixed (`cardTypeMaster` null-safety crash, `consentTypeMaster`'s 2 endpoint/DB issues), 1 cross-cutting bug fixed with corrected scope (4 templates, not a global CSS deletion), all owner-approved faithful-reproduction bugs (`encounterTypeMaster`'s HQL bug, `cardTypeMaster`'s hardcoded-status/no-refresh/typo, `deliveryTypeMaster`'s validation-copy/10-row-cap, `consentTypeMaster`'s case-insensitive check/commented-out publish/wrong-not-found-message) confirmed present exactly as documented. Nothing merged or pushed to `main` beyond the two doc-only commits (BR entry, this entry) and the standalone CSS-fix branch, per the Clinical/Billing-Tier Addendum's mandatory Step 6 gate — held for the owner's side-by-side review.

## 151. `identificationMaster` — the 5th and final batch-15 screen, built as its own follow-up after the create-flow flag-reset decision; Steps 0-5 complete, plus a parallel downstream-consumer trace of the 3 affected flags

**Context:** completed as a standalone follow-up per the owner's explicit decisions on `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_IdentificationMasterCreateFlagReset.md` (fix legacy; implement corrected behavior in the rebuild; trace downstream consumers in parallel, not as a gate). Three streams ran together: the legacy fix, the Angular build, and the downstream trace.

**Legacy fix (decision 1):** `IdentificationMasterDaoImpl.addIdentificationMaster`'s three unconditional `setIsExpiryRequired('N')`/`setIsAlphanumeric('N')`/`setIsNumerical('N')` overrides removed, matching `updateIdentificationMaster`'s already-correct behavior. Built, redeployed to the local Tomcat, and live-verified: a record created with Expiry Required + Numerical checked (Alphanumeric left unchecked) correctly persisted `is_expiry_required=Y, is_numerical=Y, is_alphanumeric=N` — confirmed via both the app's own list view and a direct `psql` read of the row. Committed on `fix/identificationmaster-create-flag-reset` (`da85e25`), pushed, **held for owner approval before merge**, per Maker-Checker.

**DB drift found during legacy-fix verification, not code-related:** `adt.m_identification_master` was missing `is_numerical`/`is_alphanumeric`/`defined_length` entirely (confirmed via live `catalina.out`: `column "defined_length" ... does not exist` and `column identifica0_.is_numerical does not exist`) — the exact three fields this fix is about. Fixed via a single additive `ALTER TABLE`, logged as `BR-local-dev-db-drift-identificationmaster-3columns`, already on `main`.

**Angular rebuild (decision 2):** implements the corrected behavior directly — a plain create that submits whatever's checked and trusts the (now-fixed) backend, no reset workaround built in. Confirmed a real `moduleId` gate (388, via a genuine legacy `resolve` block) — unlike this batch's gate-less `cardTypeMaster`, this is not a gate-less screen. Confirmed the duplicate-name-exclude-self query is correctly parenthesized and case-insensitive — no bug here, unlike `encounterTypeMaster`'s sibling HQL bug. Faithfully reproduces: the mutually-exclusive Numerical/Alphanumeric block (checked inside `doSave()`, after the confirm modal, matching legacy's real check order — neither checked is silently allowed), the Length-required gate (`isLength` is inferred from `definedLength` being non-null on edit, never a real server field), the "exits" typo, and a non-reverting optimistic status toggle.

**New finding, not previously cataloged in entry 149's Step 0/1 audit — a "stuck confirm-modal after failed save" bug, same shape as this batch's `cardTypeMaster`:** `saveIdentification()`'s `popUpFlag` is only ever reset to `true` inside `init()` (page load + save's own success branch) — no reset in the error branch. Net effect: after any failed save (duplicate name, or even the client-side mutually-exclusive-flags rejection, confirmed live during Step 5 testing — a validation failure inside `doSave()` disarms the gate exactly like a server-side duplicate rejection does), every subsequent Save click silently skips the confirm modal until one succeeds. **Update: owner chose the same treatment as `cardTypeMaster` — fixed, not faithfully reproduced.** `popupArmed` is now re-armed on a failed save too, not just success. Verified live: success → duplicate rejection → modal correctly reappears on the next Save click → success. Committed on `rebuild/phase4-batch15-identificationmaster` (`1b6efe8`).

**Step 5 live verification (all against the real, already-redeployed-with-the-fix backend):** create round-trip with 2 of 3 flags checked → all three persisted exactly as submitted (not reset to N); Length-required validation fires correctly; mutually-exclusive validation fires correctly (and incidentally reconfirmed the stuck-modal bug above); edit/update round-trip with no false duplicate; status toggle POSTs successfully (200). `tsc --noEmit` and `ng build` both clean.

**Downstream-consumer trace (decision 3, informational, not a gate):** dispatched in parallel, not blocking. Findings: **Patient Registration is the one real enforcement point** — `patientRegistrationController.js:1404-1434`/the corresponding view uses `isNumerical`/`isAlphanumeric`/`definedLength` together to gate a `numbers-only` input directive and `maxlength` on the ID-number field; when all three were wrong (the pre-fix bug's effect), the entire ID-number input could vanish from the form until the record was re-saved. Staff/doctor/donor registration and all appointment/encounter screens only use `isExpiryRequired`/`definedLength` individually for UI gating (expiry-date picker enable/disable, `maxlength`), never the character-class flags — inert there. No server-side enforcement exists anywhere. `getActiveIdentificationMasterListByOrgId` (a second "active list" endpoint on the same controller) is confirmed dead code, no caller anywhere. Overall: the practical severity of the original bug was real but narrow and 100% client-side, concentrated in Patient Registration.

**Action taken:** identificationMaster is through Steps 0-5 on `rebuild/phase4-batch15-identificationmaster` (commits `cd7928c`, `1b6efe8`), pushed, not merged. The legacy fix branch must merge alongside (or before) this screen, since the rebuild assumes the corrected backend behavior. Awaiting the owner's Step 6 review and merge approval for both branches.

## 152. Codebase-wide sweep for the "stuck confirm-modal after failed save" bug shape — 8 more already-merged screens found with it, one worse variant found, investigation only (owner instruction, no fixes applied)

**Context:** `cardTypeMaster` and `identificationMaster` (both batch 15) turned out to have the same bug shape independently — a two-click "Save → confirm modal → Ok" flow gated by a boolean signal that's only ever re-armed by a *successful* save, never a failed one, so the modal silently stops appearing after any failure until one succeeds. Two independent occurrences in one batch is the same recurrence signal that previously justified a full codebase sweep for an unrelated bug (a `$state` DI omission that turned out to exist in 3 separate files). Owner-directed sweep across ALL already-merged screens in `new/src/app/features/`, investigation only — do not fix anything found.

**Method — exhaustive, not sampled:** grepped the entire `features/` tree (153 subdirectories, 80 files mentioning `popUpFlag`/`showConfirmModal`/`confirmSave`/`popupArmed` in any form) for the specific *persistent arm/disarm gate* shape (a class field/signal declared once, tested in `save()`, flipped to disarmed inside the modal's Ok handler, and only reset in specific code paths) — not the same as screens that unconditionally reopen the confirm modal on every Save click regardless of outcome (many "master" screens do this and have no gate to get stuck at all — e.g. `bed-type-master`, `delivery-type-master`, `room-master`, `ambulance-master`, several with doc comments explicitly stating the legacy skip-quirk was deliberately not reproduced).

**Result: exactly 10 screens implement this gate shape.** 2 are the already-fixed screens (`card-type-master`, `identification-master`). The other **8 have the bug**:

1. `new/src/app/features/state-master/state-master.ts` — gate `popUpFlag` (signal), failure branch (`doSave()`, "State already exits...!!") never re-arms it; only success-path `resetForm()` does.
2. `new/src/app/features/race-master/race-master.ts` — identical shape, gate `popUpFlag`, "Race already exits...!!".
3. `new/src/app/features/short-leave-reason-master/short-leave-reason-master.ts` — gate `popUpFlag`, "Short Leave Reason already exits...!!"; the Cancel/X path (`closeConfirmModal()`) does re-arm, but that's never reached after a failed save (the modal is already closed before `doSave()` runs).
4. `new/src/app/features/out-side-hospital-master/out-side-hospital-master.ts` — gate `popUpFlag`; its own doc comment already explicitly flags this as a faithfully-reproduced legacy bug (not previously connected to the cardTypeMaster/identificationMaster recurrence pattern).
5. `new/src/app/features/reason-master/reason-master.ts` — gate `confirmArmed` (plain boolean, not a signal); comment at the failure branch: "confirmArmed stays disarmed."
6. `new/src/app/features/reason-type-master/reason-type-master.ts` — identical shape, gate `confirmArmed`.
7. `new/src/app/features/consent-type-master/consent-type-master.ts` — gate `confirmArmed`; already logged in this batch's entry 149/150 as an approved-to-faithfully-reproduce finding — this sweep just confirms it belongs to the same recurring shape as the two now-fixed screens, not a one-off.
8. **`new/src/app/features/employee-category-master/employee-category-master.ts` — a worse variant, not previously known.** Gate `confirmModalConsumed` (inverted polarity: `true` means "already used up"), set `true` the first time Ok is clicked and **never reset anywhere in the file** — not on success, not on failure, not in `edit()`. The confirm modal can show **at most once per page load, period**, regardless of whether that first save succeeds or fails — strictly more broken than the other 8, which at least re-arm on a success.

**Action taken:** investigation only, per explicit instruction — no code changed. All 8 findings reported to the owner alongside batch 16's kickoff for a decision on treatment (fix consistently, matching `cardTypeMaster`/`identificationMaster`'s treatment, vs. faithfully reproduce, vs. handle case-by-case) — not decided in this entry.

**Major update — this is a rebuild-introduced bug, not real legacy behavior, in all 8 cases:** discovered while building batch 16's `currencydenomination` (that build agent flagged a genuine ambiguity it couldn't resolve statically). All 10 screens sharing this gate shape use the SAME shared AngularJS `<modal visible="showModal"></modal>` directive (`commonPopUpDirectives.js`), not custom per-screen modal markup. That directive's Ok button — `one-click-only="PopupOkBtn();"` — is **hardcoded inside the directive's own template string**, not something each screen configures; its `oneClickOnly` click handler and a `$watch` on `showModal` both reset the confirm-gate flag to armed on every Ok click, structurally, regardless of what any individual screen's own success/failure branches do. This means the flag can never actually stay stuck in real legacy — the "bug" only existed because this project read each screen's own controller-level `popUpFlag` assignments in isolation, missing the shared directive's own reset mechanism.

**Verified live, twice, independently, against real unmodified legacy (not just static/structural argument):**
1. `localhost:8080/#/cardTypeMaster` — duplicate-name failure, then a fresh save with new values on the very next click: confirm modal correctly reappeared.
2. `localhost:8080/#/raceMaster` — same sequence, different screen, different session: confirm modal correctly reappeared, full round trip to a real "Race added successfully...!!".

**Correction to this entry's own item 7 and to entries 149/150:** `consentTypeMaster`'s stuck-modal behavior was NOT faithfully reproducing legacy — it was a rebuild-introduced bug. The "approved-to-faithfully-reproduce" framing in entry 149/150 was wrong (that decision was about a *different*, still-correct finding — this screen's case-insensitive duplicate-check defect, which is real and unaffected by this correction). `encounterTypeMaster` was re-checked directly and does **not** have this bug at all — its rebuild (`encounter-type-master.ts`) never modeled a persistent arm/disarm gate in the first place (`save()` unconditionally calls `showConfirmModal.set(true)` every time); it was listed as a candidate for correction in an earlier verbal report to the owner, which was itself an error, now corrected. `encounterTypeMaster`'s separate, real HQL duplicate-check bug (entry 149/150) is untouched by any of this.

**Fix applied:** all 8 already-merged screens with the real bug (`state-master`, `race-master`, `short-leave-reason-master`, `out-side-hospital-master`, `reason-master`, `reason-type-master`, `consent-type-master`, `employee-category-master`) corrected to the same pattern already used for `cardTypeMaster`/`identificationMaster` — re-arm the confirm gate on a failed save too, not just success (`employee-category-master`'s `confirmModalConsumed` now resets on both success and failure, not never). Held on `fix/stuck-confirm-modal-8-screens`, pushed, not merged — owner review pending. Live-verified post-fix on a subset (see this entry's own follow-up verification, not repeated here). Batch 16's `currencydenomination`/`incidentCategoryMaster`/`mortuaryBedMaster` built with the corrected (always-rearm) pattern directly, no separate fix needed for those three.

## 153. `nursingStationMaster` — 4th batch-16 screen, built after the `updated_by` audit-column corruption decision, Steps 0-5 complete

**Context:** built per the owner's recorded decision on `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_NursingStationMasterUpdatedByCorruption.md` — Step 2+ proceeds wiring to the existing endpoints exactly as they are today (option 2 in that doc), the server-side `updated_by=0` corruption on every UPDATE is a known, disclosed, purely server-side bug with zero client-observable effect, left un-worked-around; a one-line legacy fix is separately proposed and held on `fix/nursingstationmaster-updatedby-corruption`, untouched by this session.

**Step 0/1 audit — module id 547 confirmed independently** (`app.js:8575-8601`'s live `resolve` block, `arr.filter(item => item.id == 547)`), not just trusted from the orchestrating session's brief. Java controller base mapping (`@RequestMapping("api/global")`, `NursingStationServiceController.java:25`), all 6 URL-config constants, and the `NursingStationMasterDto` field set were all re-verified directly against source and matched the brief exactly. One divergence found and corrected in the store-dropdown response shape: `GET /api/unit/main/stores/{unitId}/{orgId}` (`MainStoreController.GetAll`) returns a RAW JSON ARRAY (`ResponseEntity<List<MainStoreListResponse>>`), NOT the usual `{status, listObject}` envelope every other endpoint on this screen uses — modeled correctly in `nursing-station-master.service.ts` as `Observable<StoreOption[]>` from the start, not retrofitted.

**Real finding, not previously documented in the orchestrating session's audit — a server-side duplicate-DESCRIPTION check exists on both save and update**, found live during Step 5, not from the Step 0/1 controller read alone (the check lives entirely in `NursingStationServiceImpl`'s service layer, invisible from the frontend controller/view). `saveNursingStationMaster`/`updateNursingStationMaster` (service:43-57/81-94) both call a duplicate-check DAO method before persisting — `LOWER(nursingStationDesc)` match, scoped to `organizationId`+`unitId` on save, but scoped ONLY to `organizationId` (not `unitId`) on update's exclude-self variant, a real, narrow, purely-server-side divergence between the two checks' scope (see `nursing-station-master.ts`'s class doc comment for the full citation). Returns `{status:'error', message:'Already Exists.'}` — faithfully surfaced via the existing generic failure branch, no special-casing needed.

**Confirm-modal fix applied directly** (Save only, matching `currencydenomination`/`incidentCategoryMaster`/`mortuaryBedMaster`, entry 152's established, owner-directed treatment for this rebuild-introduced bug shape, now the 9th screen on this branch built with the corrected always-rearm pattern from the start) — `popupArmed` re-arms on both the success AND failure branch of `doSave()`.

**Search-filter-is-real finding, confirmed by direct view read:** unlike `currencyMaster`'s decorative search box (batch 16, same branch), this screen's `<input ng-model="searchKeyword">` (view:108) feeds a real, live `ng-repeat="info in nursingStationList|filter:searchKeyword"` (view:172) — Angular's built-in generic (all-fields) `filter`, applied client-side to whatever page is already loaded via real server-side offset/count pagination. Modeled as a genuine `displayedRows` computed signal narrowed to code/desc/store-name substring match, the same established simplification this codebase already uses elsewhere (not a new decision here).

**Step 2 (golden trace):** not captured via `tools/golden-trace/capture.js` tooling directly — instead verified equivalently via direct in-page `GenericService.serviceAction(...)` invocations against the real, already-running local legacy instance (`localhost:8080`, real seeded session), which served the same purpose (confirming real request/response shapes before/while building) and caught a real, live backend defect (see local-dev-DB-drift below) that a load-only trace would also have missed until an actual save was attempted.

**Step 4/5 — real browser-automation testing against both the real legacy app and the rebuilt Angular screen, same dev session, same seeded data:**
- Access control: confirmed denial for a logged-out session (`/accessDenied?reason=notLoggedIn`) AND, separately, for a logged-in session with module 547 removed from its `permissions` localStorage blob (`/accessDenied`, no reason param) — both paths of `moduleAccessGuard` exercised, not just one.

- The two-click confirm modal, a REAL failed save (duplicate description "Already Exists." — see finding above, not a fabricated/mocked failure), and confirmation the modal correctly reappears on the very next Save click, all verified live on the rebuild.
- Edit/update round trip verified on both legacy and the rebuild, including cross-checking via direct `psql` reads that the known `updated_by=0` corruption reproduces IDENTICALLY on both (row 1, edited via legacy directly: `updated_by` went from 1→0; row 2, edited via the rebuild: `updated_by` persisted as 0 on creation-then-update too) — empirical confirmation the rebuild's wiring is byte-for-byte faithful to the current (broken) endpoint behavior, not a rebuild-side amplification or masking of it.

**Local-dev-DB drift found and fixed (additive, owner-approved precedent):** `public.m_store`'s `general_ledger_id` column was missing entirely (breaking the SHARED `MainStoreController.GetAll` endpoint used by every screen with a Store dropdown, not just this one — confirmed via live `catalina.out`, a real Hibernate Criteria-query column-read failure), and separately the table had zero seed rows at all in this environment. Both confirmed identically broken on real, unmodified legacy first (not a rebuild bug). Fixed via one additive `ALTER TABLE` plus one seed row using an existing, already-active `store_type_id` (the query's INNER JOIN to `storeType` silently excludes any row with a NULL/inactive store type — a naive seed insert without this would still have returned an empty list). Logged as `BR-local-dev-db-drift-mstore-generalledger-and-seed` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`/`docs/BR_INDEX.md`. Flagged there for whoever next touches `wardStoreMapper` (entry 136's prior "Store dropdown returns zero real options" finding may now resolve differently, not re-verified as part of this session).

Recovered by re-deriving the full permissions blob from a fresh legacy login and transferring it to the `:4200` origin via a temporary local file server (a `permissions_backup.json` file was briefly written to the local `~/Downloads` folder as an intermediate step and deleted immediately after use, alongside the temporary Python CORS server — both fully cleaned up, nothing committed). Flagging this here per this project's disclosure discipline even though it's dev-tooling-only and already fully resolved, not a follow-up item.

## 157. `fix/stuck-confirm-modal-8-screens` -- 3 of the 8 screens' fix was incomplete (modal never closed on failure), found during the owner's Step 6 spot-check; also surfaced a dev-server environment gap affecting all Step 6 testing on this branch

**Context:** during the owner's Step 6 spot-check of `state-master` (one of 3 screens chosen to represent the different gate-naming variants across the 8-screen fix), a forced duplicate-name rejection left the "Are you sure you want to Save?" confirm modal visibly stuck open, stacked with the "State already exits...!!" error toast, instead of closing. Investigated per the owner's explicit instruction to verify properly rather than dismiss, same discipline as the `cardTypeMaster`/`encounterTypeMaster` corrections earlier this project.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading source directly, not guessed:** entry 152's fix re-armed each screen's `popUpFlag`/`confirmArmed` gate on both success and failure, but on 3 of the 8 screens (`state-master`, `race-master`, `out-side-hospital-master`), the Ok-button handler (`confirmSave()`/`popupOkBtn()`) never called `showConfirmModal.set(false)` before firing the real save -- only the *gate* was re-armed, not the modal's own visibility signal. The other 5 screens (`consent-type-master`, `employee-category-master`, `reason-master`, `reason-type-master`, `short-leave-reason-master`) were checked directly and already close the modal correctly in their own Ok-button handler.

**`state-master`/`race-master` compound this into a worse, silent bug:** both use `showConfirmModal.update(v => !v)` (a toggle) to open the modal on Save, not a direct `.set(true)` like the other 6 screens. Since the modal never closed after a failure, the *next* Save click's toggle would flip it **closed** instead of open -- meaning after any failed save, the very next Save click would appear to do nothing (silently closing an already-open dialog the user may not have consciously registered as "open"), requiring a confusing extra click before the modal would visibly reappear. `out-side-hospital-master` uses `.set(true)` so isn't vulnerable to the inversion, but still left the modal stuck open visually after every failure.

**Fix applied and live-verified** (commit `ce04970` on `fix/stuck-confirm-modal-8-screens`): all 3 screens' Ok-button handlers now call `showConfirmModal.set(false)` before the real save, matching the shared legacy `<modal>` directive's actual close-on-Ok behavior (the same directive mechanism entry 152 already established as this bug class's root cause). Live-verified end-to-end on `state-master`: duplicate rejection → modal correctly closes → fresh value entered → Save → modal correctly **reopens** (not toggled shut) → confirm → real "Added Successfully." toast, row appears. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Separate but related finding -- the dev server was serving the WRONG branch for this entire spot-check, discovered mid-investigation:** the local Angular dev server (`ng serve`, running continuously since before this session) was serving whatever branch happened to be checked out in `new/`'s working tree. At the time the owner tested `state-master`/`employee-category-master`/`consent-type-master`, that branch was `rebuild/phase4-batch16`, whose working-tree copies of all 8 confirm-modal screens are byte-identical to `main` -- i.e. the **unfixed, pre-entry-152** versions, not `fix/stuck-confirm-modal-8-screens`'s code at all. Confirmed via direct `git diff` against both branches for all 3 spot-check screens before this investigation went further. This means:
- The owner's `state-master: PASS` report was very likely a misread of the exact bug being fixed here (this entry) -- the modal never actually closed after the forced rejection, so a subsequent interaction with the still-open dialog could easily look like "it correctly reappeared" without a structural before/after comparison. Not confirmed as certainly what happened (the owner's exact click sequence wasn't logged), but consistent with every symptom observed live during this investigation.
- `employee-category-master`'s and `consent-type-master`'s Save-confirm-modal behavior was ALSO tested against unfixed code, though their own confirm-modal implementations were independently verified correct on the fix branch (per this entry, above) -- so those two screens' Save-flow behavior, once retested against the right branch, should pass, but the environment gap means neither was actually validated by the owner's testing so far.
- The employee-category-master Update-flow duplicate-check discrepancy the owner found separately (this session) is **unaffected** by this branch mixup -- `update()`'s duplicate-check logic is identical between `main` and the fix branch (the fix only touches Save-confirm-modal code), confirmed by diff.

**Not yet resolved as of this entry:** how to keep the dev server pointed at the right branch for future spot-checks without manual intervention each time -- flagged for the orchestrating session to address before the owner resumes testing, not decided here.

**Action taken:** fix committed (`ce04970`), live-verified on `state-master`. Held on `fix/stuck-confirm-modal-8-screens` -- not merged, not pushed beyond what's already on GitHub for backup. Owner needs to redo the 3-screen spot-check once the environment is confirmed to be serving the correct branch.

**Update, environment fixed + Step 6 review (2026-07-24):** working tree switched to `fix/stuck-confirm-modal-8-screens` (this branch) so the dev server actually serves the fixed code. Before recording the owner's re-test as final, explicitly re-verified three independent ways, on request: (1) `git branch --show-current` = `fix/stuck-confirm-modal-8-screens`, working tree clean; (2) all 3 spot-check screens' working-tree files confirmed to match this branch's HEAD and to differ from `main`; (3) a fresh, real-time click-through on `state-master` performed at the moment of verification (not reused from earlier in the session) reproduced the fixed behavior live -- duplicate rejection, modal closes cleanly, no stuck dialog.

Owner then redid the full 3-screen spot-check against the confirmed-correct branch, this time specifically watching the modal's open/close state rather than just the error message:
1. **`state-master`: PASS** -- modal closes after the "already exists" rejection, then correctly reopens on a fresh-value retry.
2. **`employee-category-master`: PASS** -- duplicate save correctly triggers the modal, error message shown correctly.
3. **`consent-type-master`: PASS** -- Update correctly shows no modal (by design, unaffected by this bug class), Save correctly shows the modal on a duplicate, error message shown correctly.

3 of 3 checks pass, no discrepancies, this time against verified-correct code. This closes out the owner's spot-check obligation for the 8-screen fix (structural analysis + 2 independent live repros already established the shared root cause; this spot-check was the owner's own confirmation across the 3 different gate-naming variants). `fix/stuck-confirm-modal-8-screens` is clear for merge once the owner gives explicit merge approval (not yet given as of this entry -- held, not merged, not pushed beyond what's already on GitHub for backup).

**Known open item at the time this was written, resolved at merge:** this branch and `rebuild/phase4-batch16` diverged from `main` before entries 153/154 existed on the latter, so this entry was numbered 155 on this branch's own lineage, independently of `rebuild/phase4-batch16`'s 153/154. Both branches merged into `main` on 2026-07-24 (`rebuild/phase4-batch16` first, this branch second); this entry was renumbered to 157 at that merge to resolve the collision, per the "Merge-conflict renumbering note" immediately preceding this entry's header above.
