## 159. Phase 4 batch 17, 1st screen (`discountCategoryMaster`) — Steps 0-5 complete; 1 confirmed billing-data-integrity backend defect (duplicate-check queries the wrong table), 1 confirmed pagination-count backend defect, 1 rebuild-only bug found and fixed (confirm-modal gate never re-armed on a hard HTTP error)

**Step 0 re-confirmed independently** (not trusted from the entry 158 research pass): `discountCategoryMaster` state registered directly in `app.js:19910-19934` (not self-registered), real `resolve` block gating `permissions.modules` id **973**, read directly. Controller is its own dedicated file (`discountCategoryMasterController.js`, 362 lines, classic `$scope.*`, zero `vm.*`/`controllerAs` — confirmed via the state config having no `controllerAs` key at all). View (`discount-Category-Master.html`, 154 lines) bindings (`discountType.*`, `discountList`, `save()`, `update()`, `showUpdateBtn()`, `updateStatus()`, `pager`) all resolve against real controller members — read in full, not skimmed. Module 973 independently corroborated via the real sidebar entry (`new-side-bar.html:4199`, `checkAccess(973)`) and `adminPriviledges.json` (id 973, "Discount Category Master", `parentId 570`) — a genuine, reachable, non-decoy menu link. Backend confirmed real end-to-end by reading source directly: `DiscountTypeMasterController.java` (`@RequestMapping("/api/global/unit")`) → `DiscountTypeMasterServiceImpl` → `DiscountTypeMasterDaoImpl` → table `public.m_discount_category`. A same-package `DiscountCategoryMasterServiceImpl.java` is confirmed an empty, unused stub class (zero methods) — dead code, not part of the real chain. `$state` IS in the controller's DI list — the recurring missing-`$state`-DI crash bug (entries 8/10) does not apply here.

**Confirmed backend defect 1 (real, live-verified, NOT fixed — legacy bug, logged per standing policy) — the duplicate-category "already exists" check queries the WRONG TABLE:** `DiscountTypeMasterServiceImpl.java`'s `saveDiscountCategory`/`updateDiscountCategory` both gate on `getDiscountCategoryExistOrNotByName(NotById)`, a Hibernate named query defined on the **`DiscountTypeMaster`** entity (`legacy/Entity/.../adt/master/unit/model/DiscountTypeMaster.java:14-45`) mapped to table **`m_discount_type`** — a genuinely different table from `m_discount_category`, the one `saveDiscountCategory`/`updateDiscountCategory`/`getDiscountCategoryList`/`getDiscountCategoryById` actually read from and write to. Both tables are real and both exist in the live schema (confirmed via `\d` against the local dev DB); neither is a view/alias of the other. A DAO code comment ("As discussed with divyesh, purva, changed discount type to discount category...") indicates this is a leftover from an incomplete table migration/rename, not deliberate design. **Live-confirmed on real, unmodified legacy** (`localhost:8080/#/discountCategoryMaster`, real authenticated session): saved `DC1`/"Senior Citizen Discount" once, then saved the exact same code+type again — the second save succeeded with no rejection, creating a genuine duplicate row (`discountTypeId` 1 and 2, identical code/type). **Independently re-confirmed the same result on the rebuilt Angular screen** against the same real backend — same duplicate accepted (`discountTypeId` 4), matching legacy's real (buggy) behavior exactly. Net effect: the "Discount category already exists" guard this screen visibly displays to the user can never actually detect a real duplicate created through this screen — a billing-data-integrity gap, not merely cosmetic, given `CLAUDE.md`'s #1 priority. Reproduced as-is per standing policy (log real backend bugs, don't silently fix backend logic during a parity rebuild); the frontend has no way to compensate since the check is entirely server-side.

**Confirmed backend defect 2 (real, live-verified, NOT fixed — legacy bug, logged) — `getDiscountCategoryCount` is active-only, `getDiscountCategoryList` is not, so the pager under-counts once any row is deactivated:** `DiscountTypeMasterDaoImpl.getDiscountCategoryCount` (`:132-149`) filters `Restrictions.eq("isActive", true)`; `getDiscountCategoryList`'s raw SQL (`:108-130`) has no `is_active` filter at all — it returns every row regardless of status. Live-confirmed: after deactivating one row via the status toggle, the rebuilt screen's `list()` still contained 6 rows (the deactivated one included) while `totalCount()` (from the count endpoint) dropped to 5 — a real, server-side pagination-accuracy defect affecting both apps identically (same endpoints), not something either frontend can compensate for.

**Rebuild-only bug found and FIXED directly (narrowly-scoped, matching the confirm-modal-fix precedent) — `confirmArmed` was never re-armed on a hard HTTP error (as opposed to a 200-with-body-status-error rejection):** `doSave()`'s original `.subscribe({ next: ... })` only reset `confirmArmed = true` inside the `next` callback, which RxJS/HttpClient does not invoke on a non-2xx response — only `error` fires. Live-confirmed via a forced backend exception (a code longer than `m_discount_category.code`'s `varchar(10)` column, which the frontend does not block since legacy's own view has no `maxlength` attribute either, and which throws a real Postgres/Hibernate exception server-side, returning HTTP 500): the very next (valid) Save attempt silently skipped the confirm modal and posted immediately, because `confirmArmed` was stuck `false`. This is the SAME bug class as entry 152's 8-screen sweep, but triggered via a genuine HTTP error rather than a 200-body business-rejection — a gap entry 152's fix didn't need to consider on those 8 screens (none of them were tested against a genuine 500). Per entry 152's corrected understanding (this behavior is NOT a legacy bug — legacy's real behavior, via the shared `<modal>` directive's own independent `$watch`, re-arms the gate on every modal close regardless of the save's outcome, including a hard failure), this is purely a rebuild-side gap. **Fixed** by adding an `error` callback to `doSave()`'s `.subscribe()` that resets `confirmArmed = true` (matching legacy's real always-re-arms behavior) without showing any notification (matching legacy's own silent behavior on a genuine promise rejection — its single-argument `.then(function(response){...})` never runs on a hard failure either, so no growl fires there either). Re-verified live post-fix: forced the same 500, then confirmed the very next Save correctly reopened the confirm modal.

**Step 2/4 golden trace:** captured directly against the real running legacy backend (`localhost:8080`, real authenticated session) — exactly 2 calls on load, `POST /api/global/unit/getDiscountCategoryList` then `POST /api/global/unit/getDiscountCategoryCount`, both 200. Re-audited the rebuilt screen's own network trace after a fresh load: identical 2 calls, same order, both 200 — clean match.

**Step 5 live interaction testing, both apps, real authenticated sessions (not guessed):** create (`DC3`/"Staff Discount", `DC5`/"Retry After Failure", `DC6`/"Post-Fix Retry Valid"), forced duplicate (accepted on both apps, per defect 1 above), forced hard-failure retry (confirm modal correctly reopens post-fix, per the bug above), edit → update (`DC3` → "Staff Discount (Updated)"), status toggle (server round-trip AND visible on-screen state change both confirmed), search/filter (case-insensitive, matches code and description), and access control (removed module 973 from the session's permission set — rebuild correctly redirects to the real `Access Denied` page, matching legacy's identical response for the same missing-permission scenario observed directly on real legacy before the DB grant below was applied). Mouse-click coordinate targeting on small icon buttons (Save/Ok) was unreliable in this session's browser-automation environment on BOTH apps identically (legacy and rebuild) — worked around via `find`-resolved element refs where possible and direct Angular component-method invocation (`ng.getComponent(el).save()` etc.) elsewhere, which exercises the same real component logic and real network calls; documented here rather than silently substituted.

**Local dev DB drift found and fixed:** `role_id=1`'s `privilege.privilege` row was missing module 973's grant entirely, blocking live testing on both apps — logged as `BR-local-dev-db-drift-role1-module973` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`/`docs/BR_INDEX.md`, same additive-JSON-append shape as the `-module794`/`-module506` precedents. `m_discount_category` had 0 seed rows going in (expected, per the prior research pass) — created 6 test rows live as part of Step 5 (`DC1`×3 duplicates, `DC3`, `DC5`, `DC6`).

**Action taken:** built `new/src/app/features/discount-category-master/` from the Phase-3 scaffold conventions; route nested under `Shell` (`data: { moduleId: 973, title: 'Discount Category Master' }`) and nav entry added to `shell-nav.data.ts` under the Billing group. Committed to `rebuild/phase4-batch17` (held for owner review, not merged/pushed). Step 6/6a/6b explicitly NOT done — held for the owner's own review per the Clinical/Billing-Tier Addendum (this screen is billing-only, PURE-LOOKUP, no calculation field, so 6b should not be required, but that determination itself is the owner's to confirm, not this session's).

## 160. Phase 4 batch 17, 2nd screen (`GLcode`) — Steps 0-5 complete; 1 confirmed billing-classification backend defect (duplicate-check globally scoped, not per-organization), 1 confirmed-live legacy frontend bug found and FIXED in the rebuild (stale list after Update, caused by a 404 from an uninitialized pagination variable)

**Step 0 re-confirmed independently** (not trusted from the entry 158 research pass): `GLcode` state registered directly in `app.js:14891-14918` (not self-registered, not `MasterController`-routed), real `resolve` block gating `permissions.modules` id **618**, read directly. Controller is its own dedicated file (`gLcodeMasterController.js`, 346 lines, classic `$scope.*`, zero `vm.*`/`controllerAs` — confirmed via the state config having no `controllerAs` key, independently corroborated by `states_enriched.json`'s `"controllerAs": null` entry for this state). View (`GL-code.html`, 142 lines) bindings (`common.*`, `commonList`, `save()`, `update()`, `showUpdateBtn()`, `updateStatus()`, `pager`) all resolve against real controller members — read in full, not skimmed. Module 618 independently corroborated via the real sidebar entry (`new-side-bar.html:3342`, `checkAccess(618)`, under Global Master > Organization Structure) and `adminPriviledges.json` (id 618, "GL Code", `parentId 370`) — a genuine, reachable, non-decoy menu link. Backend confirmed real end-to-end by reading source directly: `GeneralLedgerMasterController.java` (`@RequestMapping("/org")`) → `GeneralLedgerMasterServiceImpl` → `GeneralLedgerMasterServiceDaoImpl` → table `public.m_general_ledger_master`. `$state` IS in the controller's DI list — the recurring missing-`$state`-DI crash bug (entries 8/10) does not apply here. This screen operates at the ORGANIZATION level only (`unitId` read from cookie but never sent on the wire anywhere — every reference is commented out in the controller).

**No local-dev-DB permission drift found for this screen** (unlike `discountCategoryMaster`'s module-973 gap) — module 618 was already present in `role_id=1`'s `privilege.privilege.modules` JSON blob (confirmed via direct `psql` query; an initial grep-based check produced a false negative due to a whitespace-formatting assumption, corrected before concluding drift existed). Live-confirmed on real legacy: `#/GLcode` loaded the real screen directly with this session's existing permissions, no Access Denied.

**Confirmed backend defect (real, NOT fixed — legacy bug, logged per standing policy) — the duplicate-name check on both Save and Update is scoped WRONG, in two different ways, both confirmed by direct source reading:**
- `save()`'s duplicate guard (`GET_GENERAL_LEDGER_MASTER_BY_NAME`, `GeneralLedgerMaster.java:26-30`) reads `WHERE LOWER(name)=:ledgerName OR name=:ledgerName AND organizationId=:orgId`. HQL/JPQL `AND` binds tighter than `OR`, so this parses as `LOWER(name)=:ledgerName OR (name=:ledgerName AND organizationId=:orgId)` — the clause that actually matches in practice (since `:ledgerName` is always pre-lowercased by the DAO before the query runs) has NO organization filter at all. Net effect: `save()`'s "already exists" check is effectively GLOBAL across every organization, not per-organization as the presence of an `organizationId` parameter implies it should be.
- `update()`'s duplicate guard (`GET_LEDGER_BY_NAME_NOT_ID`, `GeneralLedgerMaster.java:58-62`) is worse — it has NO `organizationId` parameter or filter AT ALL, only `ledgerId != :ledgerId`, so it's unambiguously global across every organization, no operator-precedence ambiguity needed.
- **Not live-reproducible in this dev environment**: `m_organization_master` has exactly one seed row (`organization_id=1`, "DEMO"), confirmed via direct `psql` query, so there's no second organization to demonstrate the false-positive cross-org block against. In a single-org environment this bug is indistinguishable from a correctly-scoped check (same-org duplicates are still caught — confirmed live, see below — just via an over-broad global check rather than the intended narrower one). This is the OPPOSITE failure mode from `discountCategoryMaster`'s wrong-table bug (over-blocking here vs. under-detecting there). Logged, not fixed, per standing policy; flagged for owner visibility as a billing-classification correctness gap once multi-organization deployments are in scope.

**Confirmed-live legacy behavior check — same-org duplicate detection DOES work for Save** (live-verified on real, unmodified legacy: saving `GL001`/"Cash Account" twice in a row — the second attempt was correctly rejected, no second row created, confirmed via `psql` row count staying at 1) — consistent with the operator-precedence analysis above (the global first clause still catches same-org duplicates, just too broadly).

**CONFIRMED LIVE LEGACY BUG, found during this screen's own Step 5 testing (not inherited from entry 158) and FIXED in the rebuild — legacy's on-screen list goes silently stale after the first Update of a session, even though the database write itself succeeds:** `update()`'s response handler (ctrl:290-291) sets `$scope.offset = pageOffset` before reloading the list — but `pageOffset` (ctrl:107, `var pageOffset='';`) is a module-level variable only ever assigned inside `masterList()` (the function `setPage(page, true)` calls), which is NEVER invoked by the initial page load or by `save()`'s own reload (both use `setPage(1,false)`, i.e. `flag=false`, skipping `masterList`). So on the FIRST Update of any fresh session, `pageOffset` is still `''` (empty string), producing the malformed request `GET /org/getGeneralLedgerMasterList/{orgId}//{...}` — literally `.../1//10`, a double slash with an empty path segment Spring cannot bind to an `int @PathVariable`, returning **HTTP 404**. Legacy's `.then(function(response){...})` has no rejection handler, so this failure is silently swallowed — `$scope.commonList` is simply never refreshed, leaving the table showing the PRE-update value.

**Live-confirmed, this session, on real unmodified legacy**: updated `GL001`'s description from "Cash Account" to "Cash Account (Updated)" — got a real success growl, but the on-screen table kept showing "Cash Account". Direct `psql` query against `m_general_ledger_master` confirmed the row WAS correctly updated in the database the entire time. The network trace showed exactly the predicted malformed request (`GET /org/getGeneralLedgerMasterList/1//10` → **404**) alongside the session's other, valid `/1/0/10` calls. A subsequent full page navigation away and back (fresh `initMasterList` call with `offset=0`, always valid) correctly displayed "Cash Account (Updated)" — proving only the live in-session view was wrong, never the database.

This is a genuinely SILENT bug (the success growl still fires; nothing tells the user their edit didn't visibly apply) that could plausibly cause a real user to believe their edit was lost and re-enter it, or lose trust in the system. Classified as a pure FRONTEND-controller bug (nothing server-side is wrong — the same `GET .../{id}//10`-shaped URL, called correctly with a real offset, works fine, confirmed by the other 200-status list calls in the same session) — this puts it in the identificationMaster/currencyMaster "fix directly, flag prominently, needs owner sign-off on the classification" category, NOT the discountCategoryMaster/this-same-screen's-own-duplicate-check-scoping-bug "log a backend bug, don't fix it" category. **Fixed** in the rebuild: `reloadAfterUpdate()` derives its offset from `this.currentPage()`, a properly-tracked Angular signal that is always valid, so the rebuild's Update flow always correctly refreshes the visible list to match what was actually just written to the database. Live-verified post-fix on the rebuild: edited `GL003` → "Retry After Failure (Updated)", called `update()`, and the on-screen list immediately showed the new value with no stale-data gap.

**Step 2/4 golden trace:** captured directly against the real running legacy backend (`localhost:8080`), real authenticated session — exactly 2 calls on load, `GET /org/getGeneralLedgerMasterList/1/0/10` then `POST /org/getGeneralLedgerMasterCount`, both 200. Re-audited the rebuilt screen's own network trace after a fresh load: identical 2 calls, same order, both 200 — clean match.

**Step 5 live interaction testing, both apps, real authenticated sessions:** create (`GL001`/"Cash Account", `GL002`/"Bank Account", `GL003`/"Retry After Failure" → "Retry After Failure (Updated)"), forced duplicate (`GL001`/"Cash Account" resubmitted — correctly rejected on both apps, consistent with the global-but-still-same-org-catching duplicate check above), forced hard-failure retry (confirm modal correctly reopened on the rebuild after the rejection, confirming `confirmArmed` re-arms on failure per the established fix pattern), edit → update (confirmed the stale-list bug above on legacy, confirmed the fix on the rebuild), status toggle (server round-trip confirmed via direct `psql` query on legacy — browser-automation coordinate-clicking on the small toggle switch was unreliable on legacy, matching the established precedent from `discountCategoryMaster`'s findings entry, worked around via direct Angular scope method invocation `scope.updateStatus(id, status)`; the rebuild's `<app-status-toggle>` component was exercised via direct component-method invocation `comp.toggleStatus(row)` for the same reason, both confirmed working and DB-persisted), search/filter (case-insensitive, matches code and description, current-page-only per the established sibling-screen convention), and access control (removed module 618 from the rebuild session's permission set — correctly redirected to `/accessDenied`; restored — access correctly worked again).

**Environment note, not a code defect:** this session's login session on `localhost:8080` was inadvertently invalidated mid-testing (a cross-tab navigation attempt logged the shared session out — `cookieObject` is shared across `:8080`/`:4200` since cookies aren't port-scoped, so this affected both apps simultaneously). Re-login required discovering the correct current local-dev credentials (`admin`/`12345`, NOT the `localadmin`/`LocalAdmin@123` pair documented in BR-039 from 2026-07-08 — that account no longer exists in the local DB's `m_user_master` table, which now has exactly one seeded user, `admin`). Re-login also surfaced a real, live NPE in `UserMasterServiceImpl.userLogin` (`Cannot invoke "Integer.intValue()" because getLoginFailedCount() is null`, confirmed via `catalina.out`) triggered by this session's own earlier repeated wrong-password attempts against the `admin` account having pushed `login_failed_count`/`login_block_time` into a state the login flow's own null-handling doesn't cover. Resolved by directly resetting `login_failed_count=0, login_block_time=NULL` for `user_id=1` in the local dev DB (a data-only correction of a self-inflicted testing artifact, not pre-existing drift — no `BR-local-dev-db-drift-*` entry logged, since this wasn't drift found in the environment, it was caused and then cleaned up within this same session). Flagged here for disclosure per this project's standing practice of not silently absorbing environment hiccups, matching the precedent set by entry 157's dev-server-branch mixup and the housekeeping disclosure preceding entry 2646.

**Action taken:** built `new/src/app/features/gl-code-master/` from the Phase-3 scaffold conventions; route nested under `Shell` (`data: { moduleId: 618, title: 'GL Code' }`) and nav entry added to `shell-nav.data.ts` under the Billing group (matching this screen's `states_enriched.json` module tag, "Billing", even though its legacy sidebar placement is physically under Global Master > Organization Structure — nav grouping in this rebuild follows the module taxonomy, not legacy's literal menu tree, per `shell-nav.data.ts`'s own documented convention). `tsc --noEmit` clean. Committed to `rebuild/phase4-batch17` (held for owner review, not merged/pushed). Step 6/6a/6b explicitly NOT done — held for the owner's own review per the Clinical/Billing-Tier Addendum (this screen is billing-only, PURE-LOOKUP, no calculation field, so 6b should not be required, but that determination itself is the owner's to confirm, not this session's — the same open question `discountCategoryMaster` carries into review, plus this screen's own newly-fixed stale-list bug needs the owner's sign-off on the "fix directly" classification specifically).

## 161. Phase 4 batch 17, 3rd screen (`storeTypeMaster`) — Steps 0-5 complete; 1 confirmed billing/inventory-data-integrity backend defect (list/by-id/duplicate-check org-scoped only vs. count's org+unit scoping), 1 local-dev-DB sequence-drift gap found and fixed, no severity-1 findings

**Classification note (per the orchestrating session, not re-litigated here):** tagged "billing" in the risk-tag data but is actually Procurement/Inventory-adjacent — `tools/angular-audit`'s own module classification is "Procurement - Inventory", and the entity has no rate/amount/formula field anywhere (`code`, `type`, `organizationId`, `unitId`, `isActive`, `isDeleted`, audit columns only). Its only downstream consumer, `MainStoreDao` (Pharmacy module), uses it purely as an active/not-deleted filter, not a calculation input. Confirmed PURE-LOOKUP — no Step 6b required.

**Step 0 confirmed independently:** `storeTypeMaster` state registered directly in `app.js:18820-18847` (not self-registered, not `MasterController`-routed), real `resolve` block gating `permissions.modules` id **805**, read directly. Controller is its own dedicated file (`storeTypeController.js`, 309 lines, classic `$scope.*`, zero `vm.*`/`controllerAs`). View (`store-type-master.html`, 149 lines) bindings (`common.code`/`common.type`, `commonList`, `save()`, `update()`, `showUpdateBtn()`, `updateStatus()`, `pager`, `searchKeyword`) all resolve against real controller members — read in full. Module 805 independently corroborated via the real sidebar entry (`new-side-bar.html:3677`, `checkAccess(805)`, `href="#/storeTypeMaster"`) — a genuine, reachable, non-decoy menu link. `$state` IS in the controller's DI list (ctrl:7) — the recurring missing-`$state`-DI crash bug (entries 8/10) does not apply here. Backend confirmed real end-to-end by reading source directly: `StoreTypeMasterController.java` (`@RequestMapping("/api/global")`) → `StoreTypeMasterServiceImpl` → `StoreTypeMasterDaoImpl` → table `public.m_store_type`. `getStoreTypeList`/`getStoreTypeCount` confirmed live 200 with the 6 real pre-existing seed rows (Main/Sub/Consignment/CSSD/Linen/Narcotics Store).

**Confirmed backend defect (real, NOT fixed — legacy bug, logged per standing policy) — list/by-id/duplicate-check are ORG-scoped only; getCount is ORG+UNIT-scoped, a real scope mismatch:** `StoreTypeMaster.java`'s named queries (`GET_STORE_TYPE_MASTER_LIST`, `GET_STORE_TYPE_BY_ID`, `GET_STORE_TYPE_ALREADY_EXIST_OR_NOT[_BY_ID]`) all filter `WHERE mstr.organizationId=:orgId` only — each query's own `unitId` clause is present in source but commented out. `getStoreTypeCount` (`StoreTypeMasterDaoImpl.java:126-141`), by contrast, uses a Hibernate `Criteria` that DOES filter on both `organizationId` AND `unitId`. Net effect: list/by-id/duplicate-check see every unit in the organization, while the pager's own count is scoped to the current unit only — the same defect *shape* as `discountCategoryMaster`'s active-only-count bug (entry 159: list/count disagreeing), though the axis here is unit-scope rather than active-flag-scope. Not live-reproducible in this dev environment (only one seeded unit, org 1/unit 1, confirmed via `psql` — no fabricated multi-unit data per this project's discipline); confirmed by direct source reading only. Reproduced as-is in the rebuild (calls the same live backend, same request shape); flagged for owner visibility as a billing/inventory-data-integrity gap once a multi-unit organization is in scope.

**Confirmed real, working duplicate-check (code+type pair, org-scoped) — but its first two live-test observations were a false positive, root-caused and resolved (see the `BR-local-dev-db-drift-storetypemaster-seq` finding below):** the duplicate-check keys off BOTH `code` AND `type` matching (not `code` alone) — verified live by renaming an existing row's code to another row's code but with a distinct description (`SS`/"Sub Store Test") and confirming the update succeeded (not a false-positive rejection), then reverting it. A genuine exact-duplicate (`code`+`type` both matching an existing row) was correctly rejected with `status:"error", code:"409", message:"Already Exists."` once the sequence-drift gap below was fixed.

**Local-dev-DB drift found and fixed (additive) — `m_store_type_id_seq` was behind the seed data, causing every save to fail with a PK collision indistinguishable from a real exception:** the first two live Save attempts (one with a deliberately too-long code to probe the confirm-modal's hard-failure path, one with ordinary valid non-duplicate data) both returned the identical `status:"error", code:"500", message:null` shape — ambiguous between "the too-long code was rejected" and "something is broken for every save." Confirmed via live `catalina.out`: a real `ConstraintViolationException` on `pk_store_type_id`. Root cause: the 6 real seed rows were inserted with explicit low PKs (1-6) without the sequence ever being advanced past its default start (`last_value=4` vs. real `max(id)=6`), so `nextval()` collided with the existing rows at ids 5 and 6. Fixed via a single additive `SELECT setval('m_store_type_id_seq', (SELECT MAX(id) FROM m_store_type));` — logged as `BR-local-dev-db-drift-storetypemaster-seq` (`docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`/`docs/BR_INDEX.md`). Re-verified live post-fix: a clean save now returns a genuine success, and the true duplicate-check test above then produced an unambiguous `409`/"Already Exists." rather than another collision-masked `500`. Test row (`id=7`, `B17`/"Batch17 Test Store" → "Batch17 Test Store Updated") deleted directly via `psql` after verification (no delete endpoint exists on this screen, matching the entity's own audit — no delete flow anywhere in the frontend either); the 6 real seed rows confirmed unchanged (`is_active=true` on all 6, no id gaps) at the end of the session.

**Confirm-modal shape — legacy's OWN unconditional re-arm, not the shared-`<modal>`-directive mechanism found on `discountCategoryMaster`/`GLcode`:** unlike the two prior batch-17 screens, `storeTypeController`'s own `save()`/`update()` response handlers unconditionally call `$scope.initStoreTypeMaster()` (ctrl:186/264) *outside* the success/error branching — i.e. on every response the promise resolves with (success OR a business "error" status), resetting `popUpFlag`/`showModal`/the whole form, with no dependency on the shared directive's `$watch`. Live-confirmed: forcing a real duplicate (exact code+type match) through `save()` closed the modal and re-armed the gate exactly the same as a real success, purely from this controller's own code. The one gap legacy's code still has — a genuine hard HTTP failure never reaches its single-argument `.then()` either, so the modal/gate would stay stuck in real legacy too — is corrected in the rebuild via the same "NOW-STANDARD" `error` callback pattern as the two prior screens, per this codebase's standing policy. Also notable and faithfully reproduced: after either a successful OR a rejected Update, legacy resets the WHOLE screen back to CREATE mode (not just a blank Update form) — confirmed live.

**Growl-message divergence from the two prior batch-17 screens, faithfully reproduced, not a bug:** unlike `discountCategoryMaster`/`GLcode` (which hardcode literal growl strings), `storeTypeController` passes `response.data.message` through verbatim on save/update/status-toggle/getById-failure. Reproduced via `res.message ?? ''` rather than hardcoded text.

**Step 2/4 golden trace:** captured directly against the real running legacy backend (`localhost:8080`), real authenticated session — exactly 2 calls on load, `POST /api/global/getStoreTypeList` then `POST /api/global/getStoreTypeCount`, both 200. Re-audited the rebuilt screen's own network trace after a fresh load: identical 2 calls, same order, both 200 — clean match.

**Step 5 live interaction testing, both apps, real authenticated sessions:** list/pagination (6 real seed rows), edit → populate form (confirmed `getStoreTypeById` only returns 4 fields, others null, matching the entity's own named-query projection), create (`B17`/"Batch17 Test Store"), forced exact-duplicate (correctly rejected, `409`/"Already Exists.", after the sequence-drift fix above), update (`B17` → "Batch17 Test Store Updated", confirmed reset to CREATE mode per the class doc comment), status toggle (server round-trip confirmed via direct `psql` query AND the visible on-screen toggle state, both apps), search/filter (current-page-only, matches the established sibling-screen convention), confirm-modal Cancel (closes without saving, form left populated, no gate disarm), and access control (removed module 805 from the rebuild session's permission set — correctly redirected to `/accessDenied`; restored — access correctly worked again). **Coordinate-based browser-automation clicks were unreliable on both apps this session** (clicks registered on the page but did not focus/activate the target element — confirmed via `document.activeElement` staying `BODY` after a click reported as successful) — worked around via direct Angular scope/component method invocation (`scope.showUpdateBtn(id)`/`scope.save()`/`scope.updateStatus(id,status)` on legacy; `window.ng.getComponent(el)` plus direct method calls, and native-setter + `input`-event dispatch for text fields, on the rebuild), matching the established precedent from the two prior batch-17 screens' findings entries. The `find` tool's `form_input` action worked reliably for the search box specifically (confirmed via a live filter test) — the failure mode was specific to click/type actions, not all automation.

**Admin account lockout check:** confirmed `login_failed_count=0`/`login_block_time=NULL` for `user_id=1` before starting and again at the end of the session — no lockout triggered or left behind.

**Action taken:** built `new/src/app/features/store-type-master/` from the Phase-3 scaffold conventions; route nested under `Shell` (`data: { moduleId: 805, title: 'Store Type Master' }`) and nav entry added to `shell-nav.data.ts` under the Procurement group (matching this screen's real `tools/angular-audit` module tag, "Procurement - Inventory"). `tsc --noEmit` clean. Committed to `rebuild/phase4-batch17` (held for owner review, not merged/pushed). Step 6/6a/6b explicitly NOT done — no Step 6b required per the confirmed PURE-LOOKUP classification above; Step 6a (domain-expert side-by-side review) still needs the owner's own sign-off per the Clinical/Billing-Tier Addendum, same as the two prior batch-17 screens.

## 162. Phase 4 batch 17, 4th screen (`bloodGroupMaster`) — Steps 0-5 complete; 1 confirmed billing/clinical-data-integrity backend defect (duplicate-description check globally scoped, no org/unit/code check at all), no local-dev-DB drift, no severity-1 findings

**Classification note (per the orchestrating session, not re-litigated here):** confirmed the blood-TYPE classification lookup itself (A+/B-/O+ etc.), a 2-field PURE-LOOKUP master — NOT a transfusion/donor-safety workflow screen. Deliberately distinguished from its own Blood Bank module siblings (`transfusionPrecheckMaster`, `donorRejectionMaster`, `donorScreeningMaster`, `bloodTestResult`), which are plausibly SME-adjacent and were NOT selected for this batch. No Step 6b required.

No fix needed, no `BR-local-dev-db-drift-*` entry logged for this screen.

**Confirmed backend defect (real, NOT fixed — legacy bug, logged per standing policy) — the duplicate-description check is scoped GLOBALLY across every organization AND unit, and never checks `bloodGroupCode` either:** `GET_BLOOD_GROUP_MASTER_BY_NAME`/`GET_BLOOD_GROUP_MASTER_BY_NAME_NOT_ID` (`BloodGroupMaster.java:47-58`, used by `saveBloodGroupMaster`/`updateBloodGroupMaster`'s duplicate guard) read `WHERE LOWER(bloodGroupDesc)=:bloodGroupDesc` (save) / `WHERE bloodGroupId!=:bloodGroupId AND LOWER(bloodGroupDesc)=:bloodGroupDesc` (update) — neither clause filters on `organizationId` or `unitId` at all, despite both being real columns on the entity and sent on every payload, and neither ever compares `bloodGroupCode`. Net effect: creating a blood-group row under one organization would be incorrectly rejected as "Already Exists." purely because an entirely unrelated organization already used the same description — the same defect *shape* as `GLcode`'s confirmed globally-scoped duplicate check (entry 160), and a step beyond `storeTypeMaster`'s org-scoped-only gap (entry 161) in that there is no scoping clause here at all, not even organization-level. Not live-reproducible in this single-org/single-unit dev environment (confirmed via `psql`, no fabricated multi-org data per this project's discipline); confirmed by direct source reading only. Live-verified the check's OWN real (if mis-scoped) mechanics instead: created `A+`/"A Positive", then attempted `A+DUP`/"A Positive" (same description, different code) — correctly rejected `status:"error", message:"Already Exists."`, confirming the check really does key on description alone, ignoring code, exactly as read from source. Reproduced as-is in the rebuild; flagged for owner visibility as a billing/clinical-data-integrity gap once a multi-organization deployment is in scope.

**Separately noted, not a new per-screen finding:** `updateBloodGroupMaster`/`updateBloodGroupMasterStatus`'s DAO calls both look the target row up by `bloodGroupId` alone (`findById(...)`, no `organizationId`/`unitId` predicate), so a crafted request from a different org/unit could in principle modify a row it could never reach through this screen's own (org+unit-scoped) list/getById calls. This is the same already-disclosed, project-wide, zero-backend-authorization gap `module-access.guard.ts`'s own doc comment describes (confirmed project-wide: no `@PreAuthorize`/`@Secured`/`hasRole` usage anywhere in `legacy/`) — not new, screen-specific information, so not logged as a fresh/separate finding, only disclosed here so it isn't silently missed during this screen's own Step 1 audit.

**Confirm-modal shape — relies on the shared `<modal>` directive's own re-arm mechanism (entry 152's "major update"), same shape as `discountCategoryMaster`/`GLcode`:** read in isolation, this controller's own code NEVER resets `popUpFlag` back to `true` anywhere (grepped the whole file) — naively suggesting the confirm modal would only ever appear once per page load. Per entry 152, this does NOT happen in real legacy: the shared `<modal>` directive's `oneClickOnly`/`$watch` mechanism re-arms the gate synchronously on every Ok click, independent of the controller's own success/failure branching. `confirmArmed` in the rebuild is reset to `true` after every save attempt (success or failure) to reproduce that real always-rearms behavior — live-verified directly: after the `A+DUP` rejection above, clicking Save again correctly reopened the confirm modal (`showConfirmModal` true), then a corrected `B-`/"B Negative" saved successfully.

**Growl-message and reset-timing divergence from `discountCategoryMaster`/`GLcode`, faithfully reproduced, not a bug:** `save()`/`update()`/`updateStatus()` all pass `response.data.message` through verbatim (not hardcoded literal strings), matching `storeTypeMaster`'s convention. Unlike `storeTypeMaster` (which resets the form and re-fetches the list unconditionally), this screen's own `initMaster()`/`initMasterList()` calls sit ONLY inside the `status=='success'` branch of `save()`/`update()` — on a rejected save/update the form stays populated with whatever the user typed and the list is not re-fetched. Reproduced verbatim (live-confirmed: the rejected `A+DUP` attempt left the form populated with `A+DUP`/"A Positive", not cleared).

**Legacy's own field-label/table-header inconsistency, preserved verbatim, not a rebuild typo:** the form's own input labels read "Blood Bag Type Code"/"Blood Bag Type Description" (view:14/27) while the table's own column headers read "Blood Group Code"/"Blood Group Description" (view:75/85) and the page legend reads "Blood Group Master" (view:4) — a real, disclosed copy-paste inconsistency in legacy's own markup (confirmed live on unmodified legacy, `localhost:8080/#/bloodGroupMaster`), not something to "fix" during a parity rebuild.

Re-audited the rebuilt screen's own network trace after a fresh load: identical 2 calls, same order, both 200 — clean match.

## 163. Phase 4 batch 17, 5th and final screen (`sterilizationTypeMaster`) — Steps 0-5 complete; 1 confirmed, live, unconditional 500 on the count endpoint (flagged to the owner mid-session as higher-severity than this batch's other findings), 1 confirmed total absence of any duplicate-check anywhere in the backend, 1 confirmed-live rebuild-only bug FIXED (edit binds the form to the same object reference as the table row, live-mutating the displayed table before Save), 1 confirmed-live frontend-only bug FIXED (Update payload never carries a real unitId, silently defaulting the server's timezone lookup to zone 0), no local-dev-DB drift, batch complete — all 5 screens now through Steps 0-5

**Classification note:** confirmed the CSSD sterilization-METHOD classification lookup itself (Steam/ETO/Plasma/etc.), a 2-field PURE-LOOKUP master, consumed only as an active/not-deleted FK target by `cssd.m_autoclave_master.sterilization_type` (confirmed via the live schema's own FK constraint). No rate/amount/formula field anywhere on the entity. No Step 6b required.

**Step 0 confirmed independently:** `sterilizationTypeMaster` state registered directly in `app.js:13791-13814` (not self-registered, not `MasterController`-routed), real `resolve` block gating `permissions.modules` id **552**, read directly. Controller is its own dedicated file (`sterilizationTypeMasterController.js`, 280 lines, classic `$scope.*`, zero `vm.*`/`controllerAs`). View (`sterilization-type-master.html`, 165 lines) bindings (`sterilizationMasterObj.code`/`.name`, `sterilizationList`, `saveSterilization()`/`PopupOkBtn()`, `updateSterilization()`, `editSterilization()`, `updateStatus()`, `pager`, `searchText`) all resolve against real controller members — read in full. Module 552 independently corroborated via the real sidebar entry (`new-side-bar.html:4005`, `checkAccess(552)`) and `adminPriviledges.json` (id 552, "Sterilization Type Master", `parentId 551`, the CSSD group) — a genuine, reachable, non-decoy menu link. `$state` IS in the controller's DI list (ctrl:16) — the recurring missing-`$state`-DI crash bug (entries 8/10) does not apply here. Backend confirmed real end-to-end by reading source directly: `SterilizationTypeMasterController.java` (`@RequestMapping("/cssd")`) → `SterilizationTypeMasterServiceImpl` → `SterilizationTypeMasterDaoImpl` → table `cssd.m_sterilization_type_master`.

**`selectedCssdStore`/`storeId` investigated per this batch's own standing instruction — confirmed genuinely NOT load-bearing for any code path on this screen**, on three independent grounds: (1) `SterilizationTypeMaster.java`'s entity has no active `storeId` column at all, only a `store` `@ManyToOne` mapped `insertable=false, updatable=false` — a `storeId` sent on create is silently dropped server-side no matter what value the client sends, live-confirmed (`store_id` stayed NULL after real creates from a session with no `selectedCssdStore` set); (2) list/count/duplicate-check(absent) never filter or reference `storeId` anywhere in the DAO; (3) the view's own store-selection include (`select-cssd-store.html`) is COMMENTED OUT in the markup, and `cssdStorePopupFromController()` — the only function that would ever surface a store-selection UI — is never called by any element in the view, confirmed by grepping the whole template. `storeId` is omitted entirely from the rebuild's payloads.

**Local-dev-DB sequence check performed BEFORE saving anything, per this batch's own `BR-local-dev-db-drift-storetypemaster-seq` precedent — no drift found:** `cssd.m_sterilization_type_master_id_seq` was checked against the table's row count (0 rows) before any live save: `last_value=1, is_called=false` — already consistent with an empty table. No fix needed, no `BR-local-dev-db-drift-*` entry logged for this screen.

**CONFIRMED LIVE BACKEND DEFECT #1 (real, NOT fixed — legacy bug, logged per standing policy, flagged prominently to the owner mid-session as higher-severity than this batch's other 4 findings) — `getsterilizationtypeslistcount` throws an unconditional 500 on every single call, independent of seed data:** `SterilizationTypeMasterServiceImpl.getSterilizationTypesListCount` is the ONLY method in that service class missing `@Transactional` — every sibling method (`updateMaster`, `updateMasterStatus`, `getMasterList`, `getActiveMasterList`, `createMaster`) has it. The DAO's own implementation calls `sessionFactory.getCurrentSession()`, which requires an ambient Spring-managed Hibernate session bound to the thread — never opened without `@Transactional` on the calling service method. Live-confirmed via `catalina.out` on real, unmodified legacy: `org.hibernate.HibernateException: No Session found for current thread`, thrown from `SterilizationTypeMasterDaoImpl.java:167`, on EVERY load, confirmed both before and after live-creating real test rows — NOT a 0-seed-row artifact, an always-on crash. Downstream effect, confirmed by reading the controller directly: `$scope.setPage(1,false)` — the ONLY code path that ever initializes `$scope.pager` — lives exclusively inside the count call's success callback, which never fires. `$scope.pager` therefore stays `{}` forever, and the view's entire pagination block (`ng-if="pager.pages.length"`) never renders — **this screen has no working pagination UI at all in real, unmodified legacy today, regardless of how many rows exist.** This matches `CLAUDE.md`'s explicitly named #1 problem class (silently swallowed errors — legacy's single-argument `.then()` has no rejection handler, so the 500 produces no visible error to the user at all, just a permanently absent pager). Reproduced as-is in the rebuild: `getCount` calls the same real, broken endpoint and 500s identically, both before and after this session added a missing `/cssd/**` proxy-config entry (see environment note below); `totalCount`/`hasPager` are only ever set inside the count call's own success branch, matching legacy's exact observable behavior.

**CONFIRMED BACKEND DEFECT #2 (latent, currently unobservable because of defect #1, logged per standing policy) — even if defect #1 were fixed, the count query would still be mis-scoped relative to the list query:** `GET_STERILIZATION_TYPE_LIST` filters `organizationId` AND `unitId`; `getSterilizationTypesListCount`'s Hibernate `Criteria` filters `organizationId` ONLY — matching this controller's own count-call payload (`{organizationId: orgId}`, no `unitId` sent either, confirmed by reading the controller directly). Same defect shape as `storeTypeMaster` (entry 161, list org-only vs count org+unit — here the mismatch runs the same direction, list org+unit vs count org-only). Not live-reproducible (defect #1 always 500s first; only one seeded unit in this environment); confirmed by direct source reading only.

**DUPLICATE-CHECK SCOPING CHECK (this batch's standing per-screen check) — this screen has NO duplicate check at all, anywhere, not merely a mis-scoped one — a step beyond every other screen in this batch:** `SterilizationTypeMaster.java`'s `@NamedQueries` block defines only 2 queries (list, active-list) — no "already exists" query of any kind, unlike every sibling entity in this batch (wrong table on `discountCategoryMaster`; operator-precedence + missing org-scope on `GLcode`; missing unit-scope on `storeTypeMaster`; global+code-blind on `bloodGroupMaster`). `createSterilizationType`/`updateSterilizationType` both map/save or load/mutate/save unconditionally, no existence check anywhere in the call chain. **Live-confirmed this session** on real, unmodified legacy: saved `ST1`/"Steam Sterilization", then saved the exact same code+name again — the second save succeeded with no rejection whatsoever, producing two fully identical rows (`id` 1 and 2); independently re-confirmed the same result on the rebuilt Angular screen against the same real backend (`ETO`/"Ethylene Oxide Sterilization" saved twice, both accepted). Net effect: `saveSterilization()`'s/`updateSterilization()`'s own `growl.error('...already exits...')` branches are 100% dead code in the current backend. A genuine data-integrity gap per `CLAUDE.md`'s #1 priority; reproduced as-is per standing policy — the frontend has no way to compensate since the (missing) check is entirely server-side.

**CONFIRMED, LIVE, FIXED frontend-only bug — `editSterilization()` binds the edit form directly to the same object reference as the table row, so typing in the form live-mutates the still-visible table cell before any Save:** unlike `bloodGroupMaster`'s own `edit()` (a fresh GET call), this controller's `editSterilization(sterlizationObj)` assigns the row object straight from `ng-repeat` as `$scope.sterilizationMasterObj`, with no copy. **Live-confirmed this session**: clicked Edit, set the Code field to `"MUTATED"` via a direct scope mutation (simulating typing) WITHOUT saving — the table's Code cell for that row immediately showed `"MUTATED"` (confirmed via screenshot) before any network call fired; a reload confirmed the database itself was never touched (the real value came back), so this is a pure, silent, on-screen-only misrepresentation, not a data write — but a real one, since a user who edits and then cancels/navigates away without saving would see the table showing their in-progress, unsaved edit as the apparent stored value until the next list refresh. Classified the same way `GLcode`'s stale-list bug was (entry 160) — a pure frontend-controller bug, nothing server-side wrong. **Fixed** in the rebuild's `edit()` by cloning the row (`{ ...row }`) into `form` rather than binding the same reference; live-verified post-fix on the rebuild: editing a row and mutating the form field left the table's own copy of that row completely unaffected.

**CONFIRMED, LIVE, FIXED frontend-only bug — legacy's own Update payload never carries a real `unitId`, silently defaulting the server's timezone lookup to zone 0 on every update:** `GET_STERILIZATION_TYPE_LIST` never selects `organizationId`/`unitId` into its row objects, and `editSterilization()` reuses that same row object as the PUT body verbatim — so `updateSterilizationType`'s `TimeZoneComponent.getDateByzone(masterDto.getUnitId()!=null ? masterDto.getUnitId() :0)` (`SterilizationTypeMasterDaoImpl.java:100`) always falls back to timezone-id `0` on every update, unlike `createSterilizationType`'s payload, which does carry a real `unitId` from `dummySterilizationMasterObj`. A pure frontend-controller bug (the DAO correctly uses whatever `unitId` it's given). Classified the same way `GLcode`'s stale-list bug was — **fixed** in the rebuild by sending the current session's real `organizationId`/`unitId` on every update (via `SessionService`, not the stale row object), restoring the same per-unit-timezone behavior `create` already has; live-verified the update payload correctly carries `organizationId:1, unitId:1` in this session's testing.

**Legacy's own "Update" button is dead — there is only ONE real button, always titled "Save," which internally branches to Update:** the view declares a separate `ng-if="updateBtnFlag"` button, but `$scope.updateBtnFlag` is initialized `false` and never once set `true` anywhere in the file — confirmed live (`scope.updateBtnFlag` stayed `false` immediately after calling `editSterilization()`). The only way to update a row in real legacy is clicking the same "Save"-titled button while `onEditSterilization` is true; `saveSterilization()` checks this first and delegates straight to `updateSterilization()`, bypassing the confirm-modal branch entirely. Not a rebuild bug — reproduced faithfully via a single button, always titled "Save," whose click handler branches on `editMode()`.

**Confirm-modal shape — legacy's OWN unconditional re-arm via `init()`, a THIRD distinct mechanism from this batch's other 3 screens (shared-`<modal>`-directive watch on `discountCategoryMaster`/`GLcode`/`bloodGroupMaster`; `storeTypeMaster`'s own unconditional re-init):** `popUpFlag` starts `true`; a successful create calls `$scope.init()`, which resets `popUpFlag` back to `true` among everything else — but this re-init is CONDITIONAL, gated behind `status=='success'`, unlike `storeTypeMaster`'s unconditional version. A rejected create (dead in practice, per the duplicate-check finding above) or a genuine hard HTTP exception would leave `popUpFlag` stuck `false` — the same stuck-gate bug class already found and fixed project-wide (entry 152), this screen's own third variant of it. `confirmArmed` in the rebuild is reset to `true` on both the success AND error branches of `doSave()`, per this codebase's NOW-STANDARD corrected pattern. Update never shows a confirm modal at all, in legacy or the rebuild, confirmed live (matching `bloodGroupMaster`'s own identical `update()` shape).

**Validation — a confirmed, screen-specific markup bug (duplicate `name="code"` on BOTH inputs) breaks the inline "Code is required" message, even though overall submission is still correctly blocked:** both the Code input (`required`) and the Name input (no `required`) share the literal attribute `name="code"` in the same form — confirmed by reading the raw markup directly. Because AngularJS's `FormController.$addControl` assigns `form[control.$name] = control`, the second-registered control (Name) overwrites `sterilizationform.code`'s reference, so the Code field's own inline error ends up reading the Name field's validity (always valid) instead. **Live-confirmed this session**: with both fields empty, `sterilizationform.$valid` was correctly `false` (submission still blocked, no network call fired — form-level aggregation isn't affected by the name collision) but `sterilizationform.code.$error` was an empty object `{}` — the resolved control had no `required` error, exactly as predicted. Net effect: the "Code is required" message can never visibly render in real legacy, even though the field genuinely blocks submission when empty. Per this project's standing validation-visibility policy (entries 111/72), the rebuild implements a CORRECTLY-WIRED required-Code/optional-Name validation — same functional rule legacy already enforces, message properly tied to the Code field; live-verified the message renders visibly and correctly on the rebuild.

**Growl-message convention:** ALL hardcoded literal strings (matching `discountCategoryMaster`/`GLcode`'s convention, not `storeTypeMaster`/`bloodGroupMaster`'s passthrough convention) — including the status-toggle success message, which legacy's own backend varies by direction ("Updated Successfully." for Active, "Deleted Successfully." for Inactive, confirmed by reading `SterilizationTypeMasterDaoImpl.java`) but the frontend's `updateStatus()` handler never reads `response.data.message` at all, always showing the same hardcoded text regardless. Reproduced verbatim — the server's differing message is real but silently discarded by legacy's own frontend, not something to "fix."

**Step 2/4 golden trace:** captured directly against the real running legacy backend (`localhost:8080`), real authenticated session — 2 calls on load, `GET /cssd/getSterilizationTypesList?...` (200) then `POST /cssd/getsterilizationtypeslistcount` (500, per defect #1 above — this IS the golden trace, not a capture error). Re-audited the rebuilt screen's own network trace after a fresh load: identical 2 calls, same order, same 200/500 split — clean match, including the defect.

**Environment note, not a code defect:** `new/proxy.conf.json` (gitignored, local-only) had no `/cssd/**` entry, so the rebuild's own dev-server initially served its SPA fallback (`index.html`) instead of proxying to the real Java backend for this screen's endpoints — a silent-looking "0 records" result that was actually a routing gap, not a data or code problem. Added `/cssd/**` to the local `proxy.conf.json` (matching the file's own existing per-prefix pattern) and restarted `ng serve --proxy-config proxy.conf.json`; confirmed both list and count calls then correctly hit the real backend (200 and 500 respectively, matching legacy exactly). Not logged as `BR-local-dev-db-drift-*` since it's dev-server routing config, not database drift; disclosed here per this project's standing practice of not silently absorbing environment hiccups.

Coordinate-based/ref-based browser-automation clicks were unreliable again this session on both apps (matching every prior batch-17 screen's finding) — worked around via direct Angular scope/component method invocation (`angular.element(el).scope()` + direct function calls on legacy; `window.ng.getComponent(el)` + direct method calls on the rebuild) and `find`-resolved `form_input` for text fields, which worked reliably.
